# Changelog

## Unreleased

Changed:

- **The `wgpu` renderer asks the card for memory in small blocks** ([#131](https://github.com/ivanzaida/lurq/issues/131), decided 2026-11-23, `design/decisions.md`). An engine's device is asked for with `WgpuMemoryHints::MemoryUsage`, where it took wgpu's default, `Performance`: large blocks taken ahead, which on Direct3D 12 were measured at about 180 MB of a window's GPU memory and of its private bytes, of which a UI used under 10 MiB. `WgpuRenderEngine::with_memory_hints(WgpuMemoryHints::Performance)` asks as before, for an app that keeps much on the card. The time of a frame under the new default was not measured.

Added:

- **`WgpuRenderEngine::with_instance_flags`, and wgpu's environment variables** ([#132](https://github.com/ivanzaida/lurq/issues/132)). The flags of wgpu's instance (`WgpuInstanceFlags`: validation, the backend's debug layer and the others) were the build's and nothing changed them: a debug build always ran with validation and Direct3D 12's debug layer, and a release build never could. They are the build's by default still; `with_instance_flags(WgpuInstanceFlags::empty())` runs a debug build without them and `WgpuInstanceFlags::debugging()` a release build with them. `WGPU_VALIDATION`, `WGPU_DEBUG` and wgpu's other variables are applied over what the engine was told, for one run.
- **`with_graphics_card`: the card a window is drawn on** ([#134](https://github.com/ivanzaida/lurq/issues/134)). `app::GraphicsCard` is `Any` (the system's choice, as before), `LowPower`, `HighPerformance`, or `Luid(low, high)`: a card of Windows by its identifier, as `images::card_frames_adapter()` gives it and as a decoder knows its own, so a window is put on the card that decodes and its `images::CardFrames` are drawn where they are. `WgpuRenderEngine::with_graphics_card` and `Dx12RenderEngine::with_graphics_card` take it. Where no card of the identifier draws to the window, the system's choice is taken and a warning logged; under `wgpu` the card is found by its maker and model, so two cards of one model are not told apart.
- **`WgpuDeviceRequest` and `WgpuDevice::request_as`**: a device made ahead of its engine is asked for with all an engine's builders say (backends, instance flags, optional features and limits, memory hints, graphics card). Engines share a device, and the program an instance of wgpu, where they ask for all of these alike.

## 0.67.0 — 2026-11-22

Breaking:

- **What 0.63 deprecated is removed** (decided 2026-11-22, `design/decisions.md`). Each of these compiled with a warning that named what to write since 0.63, and is gone; the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-67.md) has each with what to write:
  - `WinitWindow::on_frame`: `on_paint`.
  - `FormTheme` and its parts (`FormFieldTheme`, `FormInputTheme`, `FormCheckboxStyle`, `FormSliderStyle`, `FormButtonTheme`, `FormButtonRole`, `FormTextRole`), `Theme::form`, `Theme::set_form` and the field `ThemeDefinition::form`, which nothing read: the recipes of `ui::Field`, `ui::Input`, `ui::Textarea`, `ui::Checkbox`, `ui::Slider` and `ui::Button` (`theme.patch_recipe::<ui::Input>(..)`).
  - The palette roles of 0.61 and their fields of `ThemePalette`: `Accent` is `Primary`, `AccentHover` `PrimaryHover`, `AccentMuted` `Highlight`, `SurfaceBase` and `SurfaceInput` `Background`, `SurfacePanel` `Canvas`, `SurfaceRaised` `Card`, `BorderStrong` `Input`, `BorderFocus` `Ring`, `TextPrimary` `Foreground`, `TextSecondary` and `TextMuted` `MutedForeground`, `TextInverse` `PrimaryForeground`, `Danger` `Destructive`, `DangerMuted` `DestructiveMuted`. `PaletteColor::replacement`, which told a deprecated role's replacement, went with them, and `ThemePalette::roles` lists the design's roles only. `ThemePalette::legacy()` keeps 0.61's colors in the roles that replaced them: where two roles became one it has one color (`MutedForeground` is `#64748b`, 0.61's `TextMuted`; `TextSecondary`'s `#334155` and `TextPrimary`'s `#0f172a` are in no role).
  - `dark()` and `for_appearance(..)` of `TabBar`, `SheetStyle` and `NavigationBar`, and `PullToRefreshStyle::dark()`: `new()`, which is light or dark as the theme is.
  - `components::ActivityIndicator` is not exported: an app's spinner is `ui::Spinner`. The pull to refresh still draws the platform's spokes, and `ActivityIndicatorProps` is as it was.

  `components::Image` and `components::Checkbox` stay, deprecated as before: see the decision.

Fixes:

- **What is read ahead of an MP4 is where its index says.** A media read ahead by the share of its length in bytes that a time is, which is as far off as its bitrate is uneven: over seven videos of a site, up to 12.6 s of video short of what read ahead of 10 s was to have, and up to 44 s over on an hour-long one. Its index is read beside it as it opens, and says where each moment is: what plays until then needs the bytes of every track's chunk that plays before it.

Added:

- **Glass: an element that is a piece of glass over what is painted behind it** (decided 2026-11-22, `design/mobile-decisions.md`). `.glass(Glass)` on a node or a component bends what is behind its box along its edge as a lens does, blurs, saturates and tints it, lights its rim and lays a shadow under it; `Glass::regular()`, `Glass::panel()` and `Glass::clear()` are the materials, with the lens and the numbers measured on the system's Liquid Glass in an iOS 26 simulator. A regular glass follows what is behind it, light over light and dark over dark, and what is on it turns with it (`Glass::ink`, `Node::on_glass_look`); `.glass_container(distance)` makes the glasses under it one surface that flows together; `Glass::interactive(true)` grows under a press, is lit from the touch and springs back; `Transition::glass()` and `AnimatableProperty::Glass` take a glass to and from nothing as a material; `Tree::set_glass_light` leans the rim's light, which a phone's shell sets from the device's tilt. `TabBar::floating()` is of glass, with a lens a finger drags between tabs, and on iOS `NavigationBar` and `Sheet` are (`TabBar::glass`, `NavigationBar::glass`, `SheetStyle::glass`). Drawn by the `wgpu` renderer; the `dx12` renderer and DevTools' capture draw a glass as its blur. `RenderList` has the field `glasses`, `BackdropCmd` the fields `glass`, `reach` and `spread`, `QuadContent::Backdrop` the field `glass`, `AnimatableProperty` and `AnimatableValue` the variant `Glass`, and `TabBar`, `NavigationBar` and `SheetStyle` the field `glass`: one written out field by field adds them, and a `match` has more arms. A glass's backdrop is blurred on a copy reduced once more than a backdrop blur's of its width, which is nearly all it costs: on an iPhone 17 every bar, sheet and button of glass keeps 120 frames a second, measured by `mobile_demo`'s probe (`LURQ_GLASS_PROBE=1`, the `probe` feature). A glass takes `.backdrop_group(key)` as a backdrop blur does: the glasses of a group are put back from one blur, each with its own lens, light and shadow. `LayerBlur` has the field `coarse`. See [Glass](docs/src/content/docs/glass.md).
- **The readers of one host take turns, by what is wanted most.** A host gives one client only so much a second, so what one video reads takes from the others of the same host: the readers of a host share a dispatcher now, which lets a request a read waits for go first, then by the reader's rank, and has a request of a lower rank stand still while one of a higher rank that a read waits for is on its way (`Reader::set_rank`). A media ranks its reader by its priority (`Media::set_priority`, `VideoPlayer::priority`) and then by whether it plays: the video that plays goes before one opened ahead with no app saying anything. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).
- **`Media::set_ahead` and `VideoPlayer::ahead` say how far a media reads before it is first told to play.** It was a second, for every media: a video opened ahead (the next of a feed) can now read more where it is on another host than the one that plays, which gives one client only so much a second and gives it to each of its hosts. Once it plays it reads ten seconds at least, as before. The reader of an address fetches that far whether or not its decoder asks (`Reader::read_ahead_to`). See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).
- **`Media::seek_near` and `VideoPlayback::seek_near` go to the frame near a time that shows quickest.** On macOS and iOS that is the key frame nearest it, where `seek` has the system's player read and decode everything from the key frame before the time to the time itself. A finger that lets go of a video's line of time wants it about there, not to the frame: measured on a Mac over an hour-long video of a slow host, a seek showed its frame 1 to 3.9 s after it was asked for, a seek near it 0.77 s, which is the host's own answer to a request for a new place. Elsewhere it is `seek`. See [your own controls](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#your-own-controls-over-a-video).

Changed:

- **On macOS and iOS a video at an address is read by the app's loader, as on Windows.** The system's player is fed the bytes through its asset's resource loader (`media::apple::feed`), with the loader's headers, rule, timeout, cache on the disk, kept starts and connections opened ahead, and is given no more than ten seconds past what plays (a second before it is first told to play). It read on by itself at a host's whole speed. Measured on a Mac with five albums' pages opened from a host that gives one client 650 KB a second, a video started 1.3 to 1.9 s after its page opened where it took 2.2 to 4.4 s. While the media waits it is given what it asks for whatever is read ahead. A build without `http` has the system's player read the address, as before. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).

Fixes:

- **Every backdrop blur of a group is drawn.** The blurs of one `.backdrop_group(key)` after the first were put back with the uniforms of the last of them: only the last box showed its blurred backdrop, and the others showed what was painted before them, unblurred. Each has its own uniforms now.
- **A frame's GPU time is measured on Metal.** A profiling session read a frame's timestamp queries in the frame's own command buffer, where Metal had not written the end of the last pass yet: the end came out earlier than the start and the frame was left without a GPU time (1 to 80 frames of 240 had one, the fewest where a frame had several passes). A frame's queries are read by the next frame now, and every frame has its time. A recording's last frame has none.
- **A seek waits for its place, not for its range.** A reader of an address asked for a range sent into from the start of the range: a seek on a slow host waited for up to 852 KB before the place it was sent to. It asks from the place now: a seek in a video of 51 MB showed its place 0.5 to 1.1 s after it was asked for, where it took 1.1 to 2.1 s.

## 0.66.1 — 2026-11-22

Fixes:

- **A video iOS plays on by itself shows its picture.** iOS pauses a video as the app goes to the background and plays it on as the app comes back. lurq counted it paused for good: it took no more frames and looked no more at the player, so the sound went on under a picture that stood still until a tap, and a swipe to another page left the sound playing. It watches a player the system paused now, and when it plays again the media plays too, with its frames.

## 0.66.0 — 2026-11-21

Breaking:

- `BackdropCmd` has the field `group`, and `QuadContent::Backdrop` the field `group`: one written out field by field (a test's, a render engine's) adds them.

Added:

- **`.backdrop_group(key)` blurs glass that stands together as one.** Backdrop blurs of the same key and radius in one window or layer are blurred once, over the rectangle that holds them all: the first reads what is behind them, the others show what it read. A column of five round glass buttons over a playing video was five copies and fifteen blur passes in every frame; it is one copy and three. Measured on a Mac with a feed of such columns sliding, frames that met the display's refresh went from about 60 to 85 in 100. A glass of a group no longer shows the glass before it behind it. See [Blur](docs/src/content/docs/blur.md#glass-side-by-side).

Fixes:

- **What a frame cannot show is not painted.** The wgpu renderer drew every rect of the frame, a rect with nothing to show and those under an opaque one over the whole window included: a feed on an iPhone 17 had six rects the size of the window in each frame, one of them clear (a video player's background) and one the app's background under the screen's. It leaves those out now: a swiped feed took 4.5 ms of the GPU a frame where it took 6.6.
- **A backdrop blur under an opaque fill is not blurred.** It showed nowhere, and a tab bar of solid color with the blur of the platform's glass copied and blurred what was behind it in every frame that changed it.
- **A window that cannot be seen no longer turns the loop at full speed while a video plays.** Its surface gave no frame, and the frame was asked for again at once: a Mac window minimized over a playing video took 93% of a core. It takes 6%.

## 0.65.1 — 2026-11-21

Fixes:

- **An SVG's lines are as wide as it says at every scale.** A stroke's width was scaled to the size the picture is drawn at twice, once by hand and again by the path's transform: a Lucide icon with `stroke-width="1.8"` drawn 21 points wide had lines of 3.5 points on a Mac at 2x and of 6 points on an iPhone at 3x, where it has 1.6. Every stroked SVG is drawn thinner now, at its own width.

## 0.65.0 — 2026-11-21

Breaking:

- `BreadcrumbPart` has the part `Text`: a `match` over it has one more arm.
- `ui::MenuEntry` has a variant more, `Scroll { entries, max_height }`, and `MenuPart` a part more, `Scroll`: a `match` over them has more arms. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A press of a switch or a checkbox is its own.** A press of a `ui::Switch`'s track, a `ui::Checkbox`'s box, or the label of either, went on to what is around it: a row with its own `on_click` (a list item, a row of settings) was pressed too, and opened or selected what the person only meant to turn on. It stops at the switch or the checkbox now, disabled or not; the row is pressed by a press beside them. An app that read such a press in the row reads `on_change`. See [Switch](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#switch).
- `AlertPart` has the parts `Note` and `Head`, `AlertState` the states `AlignTop`, `Note`, `Compact` and `ActionsEnd`, `SwitchState` and `CheckboxState` the state `Whole`: a `match` over them has more arms. `DataListView` has the fields `align`, `id` and `key`, `AlertView` the fields `align_top`, `note`, `compact` and `actions_end`, `SwitchView` and `CheckboxView` the field `whole`, and `BreadcrumbView` the field `cut_links`: a view written out field by field (a test's) adds them.
- `ui::MenuEntry` has a variant more, `Element`, and `ui::MenuItemState` a state more, `Content`: a `match` over them has more arms. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A press of a radio is its own.** A press of a `ui::Radio`'s mark or its label went on to what is around it: a row with its own `on_click` was pressed too. It stops at the radio now, disabled or selected or not; the row is pressed by a press beside it. An app that read such a press in the row reads `on_change`. See [Radio](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#radio).
- `CalendarState` has the state `OtherEnd`, `RadioState` the state `Whole`: a `match` over them has more arms. `CalendarView` has the field `other_end`, `SkeletonView` the field `motion`, and `RadioView` the field `whole`: a view written out field by field (a test's) adds them.
- `media::MediaState` has a field more, `buffered`: one written out field by field must add it.
- **A window tab without a close button is closed by a person in the other ways.** `WindowTab::close_button(false)` hid the button and kept a person from closing the tab at all. It hides the button now, and the middle button, Delete and Ctrl+W (Cmd+W on macOS) close the tab as they close any other; a tab nobody closes (a home tab) says `WindowTab::closable(false)`, which also hides its button. `WindowTabsPart` has the parts `Title`, `Marks`, `More`, `MoreLabel` and `MoreIcon`, and `WindowTabsState` the states `IconOnly` and `Overflowing`: a `match` over them has more arms. A tab's label is in the tab's `title` part, with what the app puts after it. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **A finger's scroll ends when its content is at rest.** `on_scroll_end` was called as the finger lifted, with the momentum, the way to a page and the spring back from past an end still to come, and the momentum was a scroll of its own that started and ended again. It is called once now, when nothing moves the content any more, and `on_scroll_start` once for the finger and all it leaves the content doing. A finger that lifts with nothing left to do ends the scroll at the lift, and one that catches content on its way holds it until it lets go. A mouse's wheel, which told no end, tells one when the content is where the notch sent it, and so does a scroll the app asked for over several frames (`ctx.scroll_state_to`, `scroll_to_top_of`, a tap on a phone's status bar). See [What a scroll tells](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#what-a-scroll-tells).
- **A part's `foreground` colors its text over the color of its typography role.** A text a recipe's part draws (`ResolvedPart::text`) took the part's foreground only where its typography role named no color, so a theme whose roles carry colors saw its recipes' `foreground` ignored: a primary button's label in the role's color and not on the accent. A part that sets a `foreground`, at rest or in a state, now draws its text in it; one that sets it only in a state draws the role's color at rest; one that sets none keeps the role's color. A theme whose roles name no color (lurq's own) sees nothing change; one whose roles do, and whose recipes set a foreground beside them, sees that foreground now. See [Part styles](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#part-styles).
- **Window tabs in the list mode lay a separator out the same whether a tab is in the strip or not.** A tab that left the strip for the More button's menu lost the separator before it, so it came back as soon as its own width was free and left again once its separator was back; the separator before the add button came and went as the last tab in the strip changed. A tab now leaves with the separator before it and comes back with it, and the separator before the add button keeps its room in the list mode, drawn only after a tab that is not the selected one: in the list mode the add button of a strip whose last tab is the selected one stands one separator further on. The strip of the list mode is as wide as its tabs are laid out. `WindowTabsPart` has the parts of the list of every tab (`Panel`, `Search`, `Items`, `Item`, `ItemIcon`, `ItemLabel`, `ItemHidden`, `ItemCurrent`, `ItemClose`, `ItemCloseIcon`, `Empty`) and `WindowTabsState` the state `MenuOpen`: a `match` over them has more arms. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- `WindowTabsPart` has the parts `ItemDetail` and `ItemMarks`, and `WindowTabsState` the state `Searchable`: a `match` over them has more arms. `WindowTabEntry` has the fields `list_label`, `list_detail` and `list_trailing`: one written out field by field adds them. **The list of every open tab is placed by the window**: it stays 8 inside the window's edges and is never lower than 8 above its bottom, its rows scrolling, where its rows were never higher than the window less 112; and while it leads with its search field its rows abut, where they stood 2 apart. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **A `within` rule of a part in no interaction group answers to the button it is in.** A rule of a recipe's `within`, `within_hovered` or `within_pressed` (or a node's `within_style`) on a part that no interaction group holds did nothing: `within_hovered(ButtonPart::Label, ..)` never painted a hovered button's label, nor a rule of window tabs' `MoreLabel` the More button's. Such a part now answers to the innermost button around it, as to a group; a part a group holds answers to the group as before. A recipe that had such a rule, for nothing, now sees it. See [Within an interaction group](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#within-an-interaction-group).
- `CheckboxView` and `RadioView` have the field `mark_only`, `ListItemView` the fields `title_pending` and `description_pending`, `BadgeView` and `StatusLabelView` the field `tooltip_when_cut`, and `ListItemPart` the parts `TitleSkeleton`, `DescriptionSkeleton` and `Item`: a view written out field by field (a test's) adds them, and a `match` over the parts has more arms. A list item that says something `below` has `x-item` on what holds it with the row, which had no id.
- `MenuItemState` has the state `Active`, `CalendarState` the states `Cursor` and `NavAtEnd`, `TabsPart` the part `TrailingIcon`, and `DialogPart` the part `NoteKeyCap`: a `match` over them has more arms. `CalendarView` has the fields `heading`, `keys` and `cursor`, `DialogView` the field `initial_focus`, and `TabEntry` the fields `trailing_icon` and `at_end`. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu), [Calendar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#calendar), [Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tabs) and [Dialog](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#dialog).
- `ListItemPart` has the part `Subtitle`: a `match` over it has more arms. `ListItemView` has the field `subtitles`, and `CheckboxView`, `RadioView` and `SwitchView` the field `label_wraps`: a view written out field by field (a test's) adds them.

Added:

- **The system's share sheet for an address.** `app::Share` and `WindowHandle::share(share)` (`Ctx::share` from a component): `window.share(Share::url(address).title(title).text(text).at(&button))` shows the sheet the system offers an address through, as an iPhone's Share button does. iOS: `UIActivityViewController`, a popover at `at` on an iPad; macOS: `NSSharingServicePicker` under `at`; Windows: the system's share window (`DataTransferManager`); a browser: `navigator.share`. Where there is none (Linux, a browser without it) the call answers `ShareError::Unsupported` at once and copies nothing by itself, and `share::supported()` says so ahead. What is picked in the sheet is not told. Only an address with a scheme is shared; files and pictures are not yet. `share::stand_in` puts a test's function in place of the system. See [Sharing an address](docs/src/content/docs/ctx.md#sharing-an-address).
- **A breadcrumb that ends with a link, and a step that is no link** (Orchester's Files header, Edit task and Sharing crumbs, decided 2026-11-21). `BreadcrumbStep::link(bool)` says whether a step is a link over what its place makes it: `true` on the last ends the trail with a link, followed, a stop of Tab, and giving way where the row is short with its whole name in a tooltip while it is cut; `false` on a step before it makes it plain text in the part `Text` (`MutedForeground`), which nothing follows, Tab passes and the menu of the hidden steps lists disabled. `Breadcrumb::current(false)` ends the trail with a link as `link(true)` on its last step does. `BreadcrumbView::is_link(index)`.
- **A tab's own state, its tooltip, and a press of the selected tab** (Orchester's page tabs, decided 2026-11-21). `Tab::state(name, on)` puts one tab in a state the theme's recipe names, which every part of it sees: a count of what waits painted in `Warning`. `Tab::tooltip(Tooltip)` and `Tab::hint(&str)` give a tab a tooltip whether it can be selected or not, in place of a reason's, the reason staying its description. `Tabs::reselect(true)`: a press of the selected tab, and choosing it in the More menu, tells `on_change` its value again, for a page whose selected tab opens again; off unless told. `TabEntry::states`.
- **A menu's section that scrolls, items that keep it open, submenus opened by a press** (Orchester's slash menu, filters and card menus, decided 2026-11-21). `Menu::scrolling(entries, max_height)` (`MenuEntry::Scroll`, the part `scroll`) is a section that scrolls past its most height whose items stay the menu's: they close it, are named after the menu, are reached by the arrows and by typing, and the focused one is scrolled into view; in a menu that keeps the focus in a field the keys move through them and scroll the active one into view, and the section goes back to its top when what it holds changes. `MenuItem::keeps_open(true)` calls its handlers and leaves the menu, the focus and the active item where they were: a filter that chooses several. `Menu::submenus(SubmenuOpen::Press)` opens the submenus of its items on a press of their item (and Enter, Space and Right) and closes an open one on a press of it; the pointer opens and closes none. `Menu::switch_on_anchor_press(false)` and `Popover::switch_on_anchor_press(false)`: an anchor that is no trigger of a dropdown, for a menu at the pointer over a row or a popover the app opens. `Menu::entry` adds any entry. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A popover moved along its edge, and kept inside a box less an inset.** `Popover::cross_offset(value)` moves it along the edge it stands on (toward the end over or under its anchor, down beside it), besides its `offset` away from it: a popover 12 in from a bar's end. `Popover::within_inset(element, Padding)`, and the engine's `Overlay::within_inset` and `Popup::within_inset`, keep it inside the box of an element less the inset on each side. A popover's offset over its anchor is away from it, as on every side: a negative one stands it over the control. See [Popover](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#popover).
- **A list item's subtitle between its title and its description** (Orchester's setting rows, decided 2026-11-21). `ListItem::subtitle(element)`: an element of the app's on a line of its own under the title's line and over the description (part `subtitle`, `x-subtitle`), its presses its own, as a detail's are; the details stay under the description, so a row says its label, then a program's path, then the hint, then a link. See [List](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#list).
- **A checkbox's, a radio's and a switch's label over several lines** (Orchester's long labels, decided 2026-11-21). `Checkbox::label_wraps(true)`, `Radio::label_wraps(true)` and `Switch::label_wraps(true)`: the label goes on to more lines within the width the control is given, the box, the mark or the track beside its first line and centered on it; a short label leaves the control as narrow as before. A label on one line stays the default. See [Checkbox](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#checkbox).
- **A poster that gives way to the video's first frame.** `VideoPlayer::poster_until_frame(true)`: the poster stands only until the player's source has a frame of its own to show, also where it has not played yet; `false` unless told, where the poster is the picture of a player until it plays. A video opened ahead and left paused (the next page of a feed) showed its poster while a finger slid it in and the video when it played, one picture and then another, since a poster is seldom the video's first frame; it now shows the frame it plays on from. The poster stays while that frame is still to come, and for good over a source with no picture. See [A video opened ahead](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#a-video-opened-ahead).
- **A screen reads whether it is the one in front.** A screen kept under another in a `StackOutlet`, or in a tab of a `TabOutlet` that is not shown, had no way to know it, and a video in it played on, with its sound. `Ctx::is_in_front()`: whether the screen a component is in is the top of its stack and, where the stack is in a set of tabs, in the tab that is shown; `true` in neither. Read in `render`, by the screen or anything mounted inside it, it renders the component again when it changes, so a player is paused with it (`playing_controlled(playing && ctx.is_in_front(), ..)`) and plays on where it was. A screen pushed is in front from the moment it is pushed; while a finger from the edge drags the top screen, that screen is still the one in front, and the one under it is from the moment the finger lets go far enough. A component that goes offstage in the pass in which a signal it reads changed is rendered once more first (`Ctx::mount_offstage`), which is how a tab that is no longer shown hears it. lurq's players do not stop by themselves. See [The screen in front](docs/src/content/docs/routing.md#the-screen-in-front).

- **A menu that keeps the focus in the app's field** (Orchester's slash commands and mentions, decided 2026-11-20). `Menu::keeps_focus(&MenuKeys)`: the panel takes no focus and a press of an item leaves it in the field; one item is the active one (`MenuItemState::Active`, `a11y_selected`), the first that can be chosen as the menu opens and whenever what it holds changes, and the one the pointer comes onto. While the field `MenuKeys::new(field)` was made with has the focus, Up and Down move the active item, Enter chooses it, Ctrl+Enter (Cmd+Enter on macOS) asks for it aside and Escape closes the menu; every other key is the field's. A field whose own keys come first forwards them with `MenuKeys::key_down(&event)`, which says whether the menu took the key. `Popover::keeps_focus(true)` is a popover that leaves the focus where it is. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A menu item asked for aside.** `MenuItem::on_open_aside(handler)`, as a button's: the middle button, a press with Ctrl (Cmd on macOS), and Ctrl+Enter (Cmd+Enter) on the focused or the active item call the handler alone, given the press, and the menu closes as after a choice.
- **A calendar's heading with its buttons at the end, and its keys read at its panel** (Orchester's date picker, decided 2026-11-20). `Calendar::heading(CalendarHeading::MonthThenNav)` puts the month first and both buttons at the end (state `nav_at_end`). `Calendar::keys(CalendarKeys::Panel)`: the days take no focus, a day is the one the keys are on (state `cursor`), and the panel reads the keys wherever the app keeps the focus, the arrows and PageUp and PageDown moving that day and Enter choosing it. `Calendar::on_choose(handler)` is told of every choice, of what is chosen already too, so a picker of the app's closes. See [Calendar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#calendar).
- **Page tabs that keep their places, an icon after a label, and tabs at the line's end** (Orchester's page tabs, decided 2026-11-20). `Tabs::reserve_selected_width(true)` keeps each label's room in the selected tab's style, so selecting another moves no tab. `Tab::trailing_icon(name)` draws an icon after the label, the count, the badge and the dot (part `trailing_icon`). `Tab::at_end(true)` stands a tab at the end of the line, after the note and the app's element, still a tab of the list; mounted tabs count it in what the list needs to stand whole. See [Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tabs).
- **A dialog's first focus, and key caps in the app's words** (Orchester's dialogs, decided 2026-11-20). `Button::autofocus(true)`: the focus goes to the button as the dialog, popover or menu it is in opens, the main action that Enter then does. `Dialog::initial_focus(DialogFocus::Panel)`: the panel takes the focus, so Enter confirms and presses no button. `Dialog::note_key_worded(cap, words)` writes a cap in the app's own word for the key; every cap of the note has an id of the part `note_key_cap` (`x-note_key_cap`, `x-note_key_cap-2`). See [Dialog](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#dialog).
- **A card of a height of its own gives it to what is in it** (Orchester's figure cards, decided 2026-11-20). A `ui::Card` given `height(..)` (or its recipe's) gives what its header leaves to its body and what its footer leaves to its content: a child with `flex(1.0)` fills the card, and the footer stays at its bottom. A card as high as what is in it, or with a least height alone, is as before. `CardView::has_height()` tells a renderer. What is in a card is clipped to its rounded corners, as before; a test now holds it. See [Card](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#card).
- **A data list's term at the top of its value.** `DataList::align(Alignment::Start)` puts each term at the top of a value that wraps onto several lines or is an element of several lines; `Center`, the term on the middle of its value, stays the default. A theme's renderer is given the list's id and key (`DataListView::id`, `key`), and a root it builds with no id of its own is given the list's. See [Data List](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#data-list).
- **An alert in the layouts of an app's notices.** `Alert::align_top(true)`: the icon, the words, the actions at its end and the close control at the top, the actions on the first line, in place of centred on them. `Alert::note(&str)`: a quieter line after the actions (part `note`, 12 in the muted color). `Alert::untitled()`: an alert of its description alone, called by it. `Alert::compact(true)`: an inspector's, the icon beside the title alone (part `head`) and the description, the body, the actions and the note under them across the box. `Alert::actions_end(true)`: actions under the words stand at their far end. `AlertView::title()`, `note()`, `head()`, `name()` and `actions_under()` for a renderer. An app whose notices were found by `x-body` and `x-actions` finds them by `x-description` and `x-action`, the names of the parts. See [Alert](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#alert).
- **A breadcrumb's links cut at a width.** `Breadcrumb::link_max_width(length)`: each link is at most that wide and keeps its room, its name cut with an ellipsis and said whole in a tooltip while it is cut (`Tooltip::when_cut`); the page one is on gives way first. A mounted breadcrumb keeps the refs the tooltips read from render to render. See [Breadcrumb](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#breadcrumb).
- **A switch and a checkbox pressed as a whole.** `Switch::whole(true)` and `Checkbox::whole(true)` (state `whole`): the track or the box and the label are one control, pressed, focused and ringed as a whole, one stop of Tab, the id on the whole and the parts named after it (`x-track`, `x-thumb`, `x-label`; `x-box`, `x-indicator`, `x-label`). `SwitchView::whole_root()` and `CheckboxView::whole_root()` for a renderer. See [Switch](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#switch) and [Checkbox](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#checkbox).
- **Tabs with a note at the end, a dot, a count still read, and a tab a press leaves alone** (Orchester's page tabs, decided 2026-11-20). `Tabs::note(&str)` puts quiet words at the end of the list's line and `Tabs::end(element)` an element of the app's after them, both giving way first where the line is short. `Tab::dot(PaletteColor)` draws a dot after the count and the badge; `Tab::count_pending(true)` a bone as wide as a digit in the count's place, the tab busy to tooling; `Tab::inert(true)` a tab drawn as it is that no press, menu item or arrow key selects or focuses (state `inert`). Parts `dot`, `count_skeleton`, `note`, `end`.
- **A list item with tags and an (i) after its title, details under its words, and what it says under itself** (Orchester's grouped and settings rows, decided 2026-11-20). `ListItem::tag(element)` (part `tags`), `ListItem::info(&str)` (the mark of an info label with its tooltip, part `info`), `ListItem::detail(element)` under the description (part `detail`) and `ListItem::below(element)` under the row's line, which a press of the row does not reach (part `below`). The title's node now has the part's id, `x-title`.
- **A field with no label, and rows under its description.** `Field::unlabeled(control)`, whose control keeps the name it has of its own, and `Field::below(element)` (part `below`): error rows, a count, a link.
- **Key caps in a dialog's note, and a veil that leaves a band at the top.** `Dialog::note_key(keys, words)` puts a key cap and the words of what it does in the note (part `note_key`), `Dialog::note_child(element)` any element of the app's; `Dialog::inset_top(f32)` leaves that much of the window's top, over a `WindowChrome`'s bar, to what is under it, which takes the pointer there.
- **A button whose label takes its room, whose description wraps, and that tells its focus.** `Button::fill_label(true)` puts what is after the label at the button's end (state `fill_label`), `Button::description_wraps(true)` lets the description go on to the next lines (state `description_wraps`), and `Button::on_focus(handler)` is called as it takes the focus.
- **A menu of the app's own rows** (Orchester's, decided 2026-11-20). `MenuItem::content(element)` makes the app's element all an item shows, in place of its icon, label, detail and end, and the item is still the menu's: pressed, reached by the arrows and by typing its label, called by its label, checked, disabled or unavailable for its reason to tooling, destructive, and choosing it closes the menu and calls its handlers. Its root is as high as the element and never lower than a row (`MenuItemState::Content`). `Menu::child(element)` and `children(..)` put the app's elements among the entries (`MenuEntry::Element`): a foot, a section, a field, which are no items and close nothing. See [Menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A popup placed by another element than its anchor.** `Overlay::placed_by(element)` (and `Popup`'s, `ui::Menu`'s and `ui::Popover`'s) stands it at its placement of that element's box, flipped and clamped there, while the anchor stays its trigger, where the focus goes back and what hides it out of view: a popover over a status bar and at its end that an item of the bar opens. `Overlay::clear_of(element)` keeps it clear of the element's box on the side it opens on, lined up with its anchor along it. `Menu::at(point)` and `Popover::at(point)` stand them at a point of the window, as a context menu stands.
- **A hover card the pointer passes through.** `HoverCard::pass_through(true)`: the card takes no pointer, so what it covers is hovered and pressed through it, and it goes as soon as the pointer leaves the element, with no close delay: a status bar's cards of words. See [Hover Card](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#hover-card).
- **Tooltips over a frame, hung by the app, and with a hint.** `Tooltip::over(&frame)` keeps the tooltip clear of an element around the one it describes, a chip's tooltip over the field it sits in. `Tooltip::hang(&anchor, open)` is a tooltip the app shows and puts away itself (a `bool` or a `Signal<bool>`), beside an element of its own, as an element to put among any container's children; the pointer, the focus, a press and Escape do nothing to it, and it takes no pointer. `Tooltip::hint(text)` with `when_cut` is what it says while the text fits, in place of nothing. See [Tooltip](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tooltip).
- **Copies that go where the app says** (Orchester's QA copies and gallery, decided 2026-11-20). `App::set_clipboard_writer(Some(writer))` sends every copy of the app, in every window, to a `ClipboardWriter` of its own in place of the system's clipboard: a copy shortcut in a field, the copy button of a `ui::CodeBlock` and of a Markdown code block, a table's copy, `WindowHandle::copy_to_clipboard`. A copy button says it copied, or could not, by what the writer answers. `App::set_clipboard_reader` does the same for what a paste reads. A test's writer or reader on its `Tree` stays over the app's. See [copy and paste](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#copy).
- **A calendar marks the other end of a range.** `Calendar::other_end(date)` and `DatePicker::other_end(date)`, for a range chosen in two pickers of one date each: the date of the other picker is marked on the highlight (state `other_end`, `range_other_end` to tooling) while a person chooses this end. A calendar's month names and weekdays are the app's locale's (`Locale::dates`, which an app changes word by word), as the docs now show. See [Calendar](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#calendar).
- **Skeletons that pulse in steps.** A `SkeletonMotion` says how a skeleton pulses or shimmers: `SkeletonMotion::SMOOTH`, as before, or `SkeletonMotion::stepped(per_second)`, each pose held until the next and no frame asked for between, all skeletons still in one phase; `.period(duration)` says how long a pulse takes. `Skeleton::motion` gives one skeleton its own, `Theme::set_skeleton_motion` every skeleton's. A skeleton stands still where motion is reduced, as before. See [Skeleton](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#skeleton).
- **A skeleton takes the layout an app lays on a bone**: `min_width`, `max_width`, `min_height`, `max_height` and `radius(..)` over its shape's, and `flex` and `flex_shrink`, beside its `width` and `height`. `SkeletonView::root()` builds its root with them for a renderer.
- **A radio pressed as a whole.** `Radio::whole(true)` and `RadioGroup::whole(true)` (state `whole`): the mark and its label are one control, pressed, focused and ringed as a whole, one stop of Tab, the id on the whole and its parts named after it (`x-mark`, `x-dot`, `x-label`, `x-count`). `RadioView::whole_root()` for a renderer. See [Radio](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#radio).
- **Window tabs that hold an app's title bar** (Orchester's, decided 2026-11-20). `WindowTab::trailing(element)` puts the app's own after a label, in the tab's `marks` part: a count, a mark that something waits, with its tooltip; the label gives way first. `WindowTab::describe(name, value)` says the tab's route or state to tooling before what the strip says. `WindowTab::closable(bool)`: the middle button closes any tab a person may close, whether its close button shows or not, and nothing closes one that is not closable. `WindowTabs::overflow(WindowTabsOverflow::List)`: the tabs keep their width, and those that do not fit leave the strip, the last first and never the selected one, into the menu of a More button after the tabs that says how many; choosing one selects it and gives it the focus, and the window's keys reach them all (mounted tabs; `Scroll`, sharing and then scrolling, stays the default). `WindowTabs::tab_stop(false)`: Tab passes the tabs, the add button and the More button by, and a press leaves the focus where it was. `WindowTabs::dirty_mark(WindowTabsDirtyMark::AfterLabel)`: the dot of unsaved changes right after the label beside a close button that stays, both shown. `WindowTab::width(f32)`, `max_width(f32)` and `icon_only(bool)`: a tab of its own width, a cap, and a home tab of its icon alone, 28 wide. `WindowTabs::more_label(&str)`, and the locale's `navigation.hidden_tabs` ("3 hidden tabs") for what tooling is told the More button holds. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **The bar of a `ui::WindowChrome` with tabs is as high as its recipe says.** The content was laid under a bar of 40 whatever the recipe gave it; it is laid under the recipe's height of `WindowChromePart::Bar` now, so an app's rule (`.when(Selector::new().state(WindowChromeState::Tabs), WindowChromePart::Bar, |s| s.height(36.0))`) makes a bar with tabs 36 high. The board's heights stay the default. See [Window Chrome](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-chrome).
- **A media tells when it really starts to play.** `Media::on_started`, `VideoPlayer::on_started` and `AudioPlayer::on_started` are told each time playback starts to move: it was told to play, it does not wait for what it plays, and the first frame since is shown (for what has no picture, its sound is given to the output). `on_load` says it opened, with nothing moving yet, and `on_playing_change(true)` that it was told to play; nothing said when the video is on the screen, so a feed that covers its player with a poster took it away too early and showed a black player in between. It is told again after a pause, after it waited and after its end; not for a round of a looped media, nor for a seek while it plays. On the UI thread, as a media's other events, after `on_playing_change` and before `on_ended`. See [Media events](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#media-events).
- **A scroll that can be trusted on a touch screen.** A container hears an `on_scroll` event with no delta after every move a finger makes, every step of its momentum and every step of a list on its way to a page, the last one with the content where it stays: a list that snapped to its next page after a short drag told nothing at all, so an app that kept the shown page from `on_scroll` kept the old one, and its video played on under the new page.
- `on_page_change(|page: usize| ..)` on a scroll container: the page its content came to rest on, a page being as long as its viewport, called once when it changes, whatever took the content there: a finger and the snap after it, a wheel, the keys, the bar, an offset the app set. `ScrollState::rested_page()` reads it, and `ScrollState::page_signal()` is the same as a signal for a render. `ScrollState::page()` is still the page nearest the offset that is drawn.
- `ScrollState::target_page()`: the page the content is going to. For a list that scrolls by pages (`set_paging(true)`) it is known as the finger lifts, before anything moved, so a feed can open its next video then. `ScrollState::moving()`: whether something moves the content or holds it.
- `ScrollEvent::source`: what moves the content, a `ScrollSource`: `Finger`, `Wheel`, `Momentum`, `Snap`, `Scrollbar`, `Keyboard`, `Autoscroll` or `Code`.
- **A video player's own layer knows the time.** `VideoPlayer::child_with(|playback| ..)` builds a layer of the app's from what the player is doing each time it is rendered: a `VideoPlayback` with `position`, `duration`, `buffered`, `playing`, `waiting`, `ended`, `failed`, `opening`, `poster_shown`, `volume`, `muted`, `rate` and `progress()`, and `seek(seconds)`, `set_playing(bool)`, `set_muted(bool)`, which do what the player's own controls do. With a [source](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#playing-a-video) a player's time is the media's and no signal of the app's follows it, so a feed's own controls (`controls(false)`) had no way to draw a seek line that moved, or to seek: `SeekBar::controlled(playback.position, ..)` and `MediaTime::new(playback.position, playback.duration)` now work there. See [your own controls over a video](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#your-own-controls-over-a-video).
- `VideoPlayer::background(color)`: what is behind the picture where the picture does not cover the box (beside a wide video in an upright box, and before the first frame). Black unless told; a clear color shows what the app has under the player.
- `Media::has_frame()` and `Media::is_open()`: whether a media's picture shows a frame of it yet, and whether it opened.
- `Loader::read_blocks(first, most)`: how much of an address one request of a reader asks for, at first and at most (see Fixes). `Reader::read_ahead(bool)`: whether a reader of an address fetches what follows a read before it is asked for.
- With `lurq::resources=debug` every range request of a reader of an address is a line of the log: the range, the bytes and the milliseconds.
- **What is on the screen loads first.** Of the loads that wait for a free thread of the loader, the next one read is the one of the highest priority, and among those the one asked for last, where they were read in the order they came. `Load::set_priority(LoadPriority::High)` and `with_priority(..)` put a load before the others (`Low` after them), and can be told again while it waits; a mounted `ui::Picture` gives its load `High` while any of its box shows. A load nobody waits for any more is not started at all: it was started when its turn came and stopped at its first step, after the request was sent, so a long list scrolled past held the pool for a request each. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).
- **A connection opened ahead, and the start of an address kept.** `Loader::connect(address)` opens a connection to the host of an address before the first request to it, away from the caller: an app that knows the host of a video a moment before it makes the player spares the player the handshake. It asks for the head of what is at the address (`HEAD`, with the rule's headers) and keeps the connection whatever the answer; a host asked in the last ten seconds is not asked again, and nothing is sent to an address the loader does not allow. `Loader::keep_starts(n)`: of how many addresses the start a reader read (its first ranges whole, 2 MiB at most, for five minutes) is kept in memory once the reader is dropped; 8 unless told, 0 for none. A reader of one of them starts with it, with no request, so a video closed and opened again a moment later opens at once; `Loader::forget` lets go of the start of a source. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).
- **What scrolls and what plays is read from the tree.** To what reads the tree over the MCP server (`lurq_inspect`, `lurq_read_tree` and the lookups) a scroll container whose content overflows says `scroll_offset` (logical pixels, across then down) and, while something has its content (a finger, its momentum, a way to a page, a spring back, a wheel's scroll, a drag of its bar), `scroll_moving=true`; one that scrolls by pages or whose page something follows says `scroll_page` and `scroll_target_page` too. A `ui::VideoPlayer` and a `ui::AudioPlayer` say, there and in DevTools, `media_state` (`opening`, `playing`, `paused`, `waiting`, `ended` or `failed`), `media_position` and `media_duration` in seconds, and `media_source` where they play a source (the file, the address or how many bytes, never the headers). Which of two lists a gesture moved, and which of a feed's videos plays, no longer needs a log of the app's own. Nothing of it is in a build without `mcp` or `devtools` ([MCP](docs/src/content/docs/mcp.md#what-scrolls-and-what-plays)).
- **The root is created in the system's look.** `App::read_system_appearance()`, called before the root is mounted, asks the system whether it is light or dark with no window and gives it to the theme: the root's `create` and its first render are in the look the window will have, where they were in the light palette on a dark system and a color read there and kept stayed light (0.64.0 said so in the docs and fixed the one large title; this is the cause). `theme.appearance()` and `ctx.window().system.appearance` say it from the first render. `lurq::app::system_appearance()` is the reading alone: the setting on Windows, the person's defaults on macOS, the main screen's traits on iOS, `prefers-color-scheme` in a browser, `None` on Linux. Not done by `App::new()`: a test is in the light look on every machine ([The System's Settings](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#the-systems-settings)).
- **A pager.** `components::Pager::new(count, |ctx, index| page)` shows pages one at a time, each as large as the pager, down (`vertical()`, the default) or across (`horizontal()`): a feed of videos, a strip of an album's pictures in a page of it. Which page it is at is an index the app holds (`index(Signal<usize>)`): the pager goes to the page it says, and a person's move sets it once the page is at rest, when `on_index_change` is called. Only the page shown and the ones beside it are built (`beside(n)`), each in a component of its own that is dropped when the pager is further away: a feed of a thousand videos holds three. A finger turns one page for a gesture along the pager's own way, a wheel one for a notch and a touchpad's swipe one, and the arrow keys one (Page Up, Page Down, Home and End in a pager that goes down; `keys(false)` turns them off) ([A pager](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#a-pager)).
- The `lurq::scroll` tracing target tells at debug when a finger's scroll starts, the page a list is sent to at the lift, each change of the page and where the content comes to rest, with what moved it.
- **How much of an element shows.** `ElementRef::observe_visibility(VisibilityOptions, observer)` is lurq's `IntersectionObserver`: the observer is told a `Visibility` (`ratio`, `visible`, `visible_rect`, `rect`, `root_rect`) at the first layout and then each time the share of the element that shows crosses one of the options' `thresholds`, for as long as the `VisibilityObserver` it gives is kept. The element is measured against the scroll container nearest around it, as much of it as the window shows, or against `.root(&element)`; `.margin(px)` and `.margins(across, down)` grow that root first, as `rootMargin` does. It is reckoned from the layout at every pass, so it is right whatever moved the content: a finger, a snap, a wheel or the app's own `set_scroll`. The page of a feed that fills its viewport is the current one, a thumbnail near the viewport loads its picture, a mark at a list's end loads the next page. See [How much of an element shows](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#how-much-of-an-element-shows).
- `Tag::on_remove_event(|event: &TagRemoveEvent| ..)`: the remove handler given what took the tag away, the press of its button (`TagRemoveEvent::Press`) or Backspace or Delete on it (`Key`), which it stops (`stop_propagation()`) so that a row a press opens around the tag hears nothing. `on_remove(|| ..)` is as it was. See [Tag](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tag).
- `Badge::truncate(true)`: a badge that gives way where it has less room than its word, down to its dot, its icon and its padding, the word ending in an ellipsis; tooling is still told the whole word. See [Badge](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#badge).
- **Window tabs keep a gap between their tabs, and fit by it.** The gap of the `List` part stands between two tabs and before the More button, the gap of the `Root` part before the add button, and the strip counts both: in the list mode a tab that left comes back as soon as the room it took, its gap and its separator, is there again (Orchester's title bar, 2 apart). A separator the recipe makes 0 wide is not laid out. `WindowTab::fit_content(max)`: a tab as wide as what it shows, its icon, label, marks and close slot, up to `max`, where its label ends in an ellipsis, with no measuring of the app's; in the list mode it keeps that width and leaves the strip whole, and in the scroll mode it gives way to the recipe's least width. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **Window tabs whose More button lists every open tab** (Orchester's tab list, decided 2026-11-20). `WindowTabs::menu(WindowTabsMenu::All)`: in the list mode the More button opens the list of every open tab in the strip's order, not only the hidden ones, with a search field at its head once it holds more than eight, an eye struck through on each tab not in the strip, a check on the selected one, and a close button on each row; Up and Down move through it, Enter chooses, the middle button and Ctrl+W (Cmd+W on macOS) on a row close its tab, Escape empties the search and then closes it. `WindowTabs::list_shortcut(true)`: Ctrl+Shift+A (Cmd+Shift+A) opens and closes the More button's menu, in either mode. The More button has a tooltip that says how many tabs left, with those keys, points its chevron up while its menu is open, and puts the strip in the new state `WindowTabsState::MenuOpen`, which the recipe draws pressed. New parts `Panel`, `Search`, `Items`, `Item`, `ItemIcon`, `ItemLabel`, `ItemHidden`, `ItemCurrent`, `ItemClose`, `ItemCloseIcon`, `Empty`, and the locale's `navigation.open_tabs`, `search_tabs`, `no_tab_matches` and `not_in_strip` in six languages. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **The list of every window tab is the app's to know, place and word** (Orchester's title bar, decided 2026-11-20). `WindowTabs::menu_open(Signal<bool>)`: whether the More button's menu is open is the app's signal, and `on_menu_open_change(bool)` hears a person open or close it. While the list has the focus its keys are its own: Ctrl+W (Cmd+W), Enter, Escape, the arrows and every key without Ctrl, Alt or Cmd are not heard by the page under it, so the window's Ctrl+W does not close the selected tab as well and a dialog's Enter under the list does not confirm it. `WindowTab::list_label(&str)` and `list_detail(&str)`: what a tab's row says in place of its label and a quiet text after it (part `ItemDetail`), both searched; `WindowTab::list_trailing(element)`: the app's elements in the row (part `ItemMarks`). `WindowTabs::list_clear_of(element)` stands the list 4 under the title bar in place of the More button, and `list_inset(f32)` keeps it that far inside the window (8). The row of the selected tab, or the first that matches the search, is scrolled into view as the list opens and as the search changes. `WindowTabs::more_tooltip(Fn(usize) -> String)`: the More button's words for how many are hidden. See [Window Tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#window-tabs).
- **An element that hears keys first.** `.keys_first(true)`: while the focus is on the element or in it, it and what is in it are told of a key before the rest of the tree, and a key they stop (`stop_propagation`) the rest does not hear; what the window does with the key by default is done as before. A panel in a layer over the page, which comes after the page in the tree, takes its Enter and its Escape before a dialog under it. See [Keys heard first](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#keys-heard-first).
- **A checkbox's and a switch's element ref, and a mark alone for a row pressed as a whole** (Orchester's toggles, decided 2026-11-20). `Checkbox::element_ref` and `Switch::element_ref`, as `Radio::element_ref`: the ref is on what is pressed and focused, the box or the track, or the whole where it is `whole`, so a screen can move the focus to it. `Checkbox::mark_only(true)` and `Radio::mark_only(true)` draw the box with its check or dash, or the round mark with its dot, in their recipes' parts and states, with no press, no focus, no stop of Tab and no label, hidden from tooling: the row around them is the control and says it is checked. The id and the element ref are on the box or the mark (`x-indicator`, `x-dot` after it); `CheckboxView::mark_alone()` and `RadioView::mark_alone()` for a renderer. See [Checkbox](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#checkbox) and [Radio](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#radio).
- **A breadcrumb's page says its whole name while it is cut.** The page one is on, cut where the row is short, has a tooltip of its whole name that shows only while it is cut, in every breadcrumb, built or mounted, as a link cut at `link_max_width` has. The ids of the separators are the part-ids rule's, `x-separator`, `x-separator-2`, ...: written in the docs and held by a test. See [Breadcrumb](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#breadcrumb).
- **A list item whose title and description are still read, and the frame of a row that says something below it** (Orchester's settings rows, decided 2026-11-20). `ListItem::title_pending(true)` and `description_pending(true)` put a skeleton's bone in place of the words, as wide and as high as the new parts `TitleSkeleton` (140 by 12) and `DescriptionSkeleton` (220 by 10) say, on a line of the text's height so that the words that come move nothing; the row says `a11y_busy`. A row that says something `below` is held with it in the new part `Item`, `x-item`; the row's id stays on the row, what is pressed. `ListItemView::bone(text, bone)` for a renderer. See [List](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#list).
- **A badge's and a status label's word in a tooltip while it is cut.** `Badge::tooltip_when_cut(true)` and `StatusLabel::tooltip_when_cut(true)` say the whole word in a tooltip that shows only while the last layout cut it; `word_ref(ElementRef)` on either refers to the word's text, whose `text_cut()` a tooltip of the app's own reads (`Tooltip::when_cut(&word)`, with a hint). See [Badge](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#badge) and [Status Label](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#status-label).
- **How far a skeleton's pulse dims.** `SkeletonMotion::dim_to(0.55)`: the opacity a pulse dims to at its middle, in hundredths between 0 and 1, 0.5 unless told (`dimmed()` reads it); a theme gives it with the rest of the motion, a skeleton with its own. Skeletons that dim alike pulse in one phase. See [Skeleton](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#skeleton).

Fixes:

- **An iPhone's share sheet comes up from the bottom of the screen.** `WindowHandle::share` on an iPhone showed the system's sheet as a narrow card at the screen's right edge, running off it, as for an app on its side: winit's window is made before its scene, and put in it with no orientation of its own, which the sheet (drawn by the system in a process of its own) takes. The window is given its controller again once it is in its scene, and takes the scene's orientation.
- **A video draws the window for each of its frames, not at every refresh.** A window with a video in it, playing or paused, was drawn at the display's rate with all that lay under and over it, since a video's frames were a picture that changes by itself and the shell presented from every turn of the loop. A video's frames (on the card on macOS, iOS and Windows, and a streaming image) now wake the window as they come, and it is drawn again only for a frame it has not drawn: a video of 30 frames a second beside nothing that moves is drawn 30 times a second, and a paused one not at all. A GIF keeps its pacing. See [Frames](docs/src/content/docs/ios.md#frames).
- **The system's player reads ahead only so far.** On macOS and iOS a playing video was read to its end as fast as the host gave it, and a paused one opened ahead read on too. It reads 1 s ahead until it is first told to play and 10 s once it plays. See [What a video reads](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md).
- **A blurred element that holds still is not painted again.** The wgpu renderer painted and blurred an element with `.blur(...)` in every frame the window drew. It keeps the blurred pixels now, as it keeps a backdrop blur's, and composites them again while the element holds what it held and stands where it stood: a blurred picture under a playing video costs one draw a frame. See [When A Layer Is Blurred Again](docs/src/content/docs/blur.md#when-a-layer-is-blurred-again).
- **The list of every open tab opens with no tab hidden.** `WindowTabs::list_shortcut` and `menu_open` with `WindowTabsMenu::All` were said to open the list at any time, but it hung from the More button, which is laid out only while a tab has left the strip, so with every tab in it nothing opened on screen. The list opens now, the More button still hidden, hanging from the tabs: 4 under them (or under what `list_clear_of` names), its end at theirs.
- **An element of a dialog's note wraps.** An element given by `Dialog::note_child` kept its width and ran out of the note where the note was short. It gives way now, and is never wider than the note, so a long reason goes on to the next lines.
- **A sheet's content is as high as what of the sheet shows, between two of its heights too.** A `Sheet` with several detents kept its content as high as the detent it rested at while a finger moved it: with `[Fraction(0.7), Large]` and a content that fills the sheet, the bar at the content's bottom went up with the sheet, the panel's own color under it; at the lift the content was at once as high as the detent it was going to, so the bar jumped under the window's edge and came back with the spring, and the same on the way down. The content is now as high as what of the sheet is in the window: under a finger at once, and on the way to a height by the same curve as the sheet's top, so what stands at its bottom stays at the window's bottom. Not higher than the highest detent, past which a finger only pulls the sheet a little, nor lower than the lowest. A sheet as high as its content (`SheetDetent::Content`) is as it was.
- **A control keeps its own name in a field with no label.** A radio or a switch with its label hidden took the empty label of a field that had none, and a select, a listbox, a group of radios or checkboxes, a segmented control, a toggle group, a chip group, a rating, a slider of angles, a signature pad, a swatch picker, a month picker and a file upload were named nothing by it; they keep their own now. A drop zone given a label of its own took the field's in its place: it keeps its own.
- **A list on its way to a page tells the page when it has arrived to the eye.** `on_page_change` and `ScrollState::page_signal()` changed when the spring that brings a list to its page was still, under half a pixel: 0.65 s after a lift a page away, of which the last quarter of a second is a tail nobody sees, and a feed that plays the video of the page stood still for it. The page is told once the list is within a fiftieth of a page of it (0.42 s for the same lift, less for a throw); `on_scroll` goes on for the steps that are left and `on_scroll_end` is at the rest, as before. A finger that catches the list in those last pixels and takes it back has the page told again where it rests.
- **An element's bounds over MCP are where it is drawn.** `lurq_read_tree`, the lookups, `lurq_inspect` and what acts on a `ref` took an element's box from the layout, before any transform: for an element inside a parent moved with `Transform2D::translate` (a strip of pages an app moves itself) the outline said `@609,1121` for a slider drawn at `24,1121`, `lurq_screenshot` with its `ref` said it had no visible area in the viewport, and a press on its `ref` went to the place the layout had it at. The bounds are the box with the element's own transform and those of what it is in, as hit testing composes them; a box that is turned or scaled is the upright box around what is drawn.
- **Children handed to a component are shown as they are when it renders again by itself.** The children a component is handed (`ctx.mount_with`, read with `ctx.children()`) are the output of the components the parent mounted for it, as it was at the parent's render. A child that then changed by itself went to the tree at once, but a holder that rendered again alone after that (a row that follows a finger, a sheet on its way, a pager of the app's own while it is dragged) built itself of what it had been handed, and the screen showed the child as it was when the parent last rendered: an album back at its first picture, with its own state right all along. A holder about to render alone now takes the latest output of every component among its children that rendered since. Components the holder's parent mounted, at any depth; one handed down from further up is as before.
- **A gesture that began is told to its end when what it began on is built anew.** An element with `on_pan` whose handler, at the pan's start, changed a signal that rendered it again in another shape (a seek line that gets a thumb and a time while it is held, with something new before it) heard no `Change` and no `End` of that pan, nor its touch's end: with no id and no key it was another node after the render, and the gesture's events went to the ids of the nodes the finger had landed on. The line stayed held for good unless the drag began on a node that was there before and after. A touch and the gesture of its fingers now go on for the elements that stand where the ones they began on stood (those under the point the finger landed on, inside what the render left standing), laid out first where no frame came between two events. What only moved under the finger keeps its gesture, as before.
- **A picture with a ratio and no width is as wide as its room in a row too.** `ui::Picture::aspect_ratio(..)` with neither side said made the box as wide as its room in a column, where a child is stretched, and in a row left it as wide as its pixels: nothing at all while they were not loaded, which is when the ratio is there to hold the box. It is as wide as its room wherever it is, as `width(Length::Fill)` makes it, and as the docs said.
- **`lurq_wait` waits for what a finger left moving.** Its idle was "nothing to draw and no animation", and the momentum of a `lurq_touch`, a list on its way to a page and content springing back are none of those: they are stepped between frames. A wait right after a swipe answered at once, with the list still between two pages, and a script had to sleep by the clock. Idle is now also nothing a finger left moving.
- **A video says how far it is fetched, and not that it is all loaded.** A player with a source drew its whole seek bar as loaded from the moment the video opened, and `VideoPlayback::buffered` was the video's length: a video of an hour at an address was "loaded" a second after it opened, with a megabyte of it fetched. `MediaState::buffered` now says how far the media is fetched from where it plays, and the players show that unless the app says how far. All of a file, of bytes and of a sound loaded whole. Of an address the app's loader reads as it plays (Windows), the end of the ranges it holds in a row from the position, by the share of its bytes: it grows as ranges come, and after a seek to a part not fetched it is no further than there. `None` where it is not known (before it opened; an address the system's own player reads, on macOS, iOS and in a browser), and nothing is then drawn as loaded. An audio player's bar the same. See [Playing a video](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#playing-a-video).
- **A list that scrolls by pages always comes to rest on a page.** A strip of pictures that scrolls across by pages, in a feed that scrolls down by pages, stayed between two pictures after a drag on a slant: the finger's gesture was the feed's, the strip took the sideways part of the travel all the same, and only the feed was sent to a page at the lift. A finger's gesture on a list that scrolls one way now takes that way's part of the travel and none of the other, from the finger's first points to the lift, and no list inside it takes what is left: a strip does not creep sideways under a finger that scrolls its page. A list that scrolls both ways follows the finger as before. Whatever a gesture did move beside its own list is sent to its nearest page when the gesture is over. See [Scrolling](docs/src/content/docs/ios.md#scrolling).
- A list on its way to a page that a finger stopped, when the finger then scrolled another list, stayed between two pages for good: it goes on to the page it was going to, beside that gesture. One a tap on the status bar interrupts is put on its page.
- A list that scrolls by pages and comes to its last page on its way there after the finger lifted reaches its bottom: `on_scroll_reach_bottom` (and `on_scroll_reach_top` on the first) was called only where the finger itself, or its momentum, brought the content to the end, so a feed that loads more at its end did not on a short flick.
- `on_scroll_reach_top` and `on_scroll_reach_bottom` say what moved the content there (`event.source`).
- **A video's first frame comes sooner from a slow host.** A reader of an address asked for 1 MiB at a time, so a video's first frame waited for the first megabyte: two seconds on a host that sends 600 KB a second. The first two requests are 256 KiB now, each one after them twice the one before up to 1 MiB (`Loader::read_blocks` says others); 256 KiB is what Media Foundation reads of a file at a time, so opening is one request. An MP4 file whose index is at its end has the ranges of the index asked for as it opens, beside its start, and not after it.
- **A video opens sooner from a host that gives one client so much a second.** A reader of an address (`Loader::open`, what a video on Windows reads) gave a read its bytes only once the whole range they are in had come: a decoder that needed the first 40 KB of a range of 1 MiB waited for the megabyte. And it read ahead by asking for the two ranges that follow at once, each on a connection of its own, while a read waited for the range before them: on a host that gives a client 630 KB a second in all, however many connections it has, they shared that with the range that was waited for, and each paid a new handshake while the connection that had just read the first range stood idle. A read is now given its bytes as they come; the requests go one after the other over one connection, the range a read waits for first and what is read ahead only while no read waits; a reader told to read ahead from its start asks for its small first ranges in one request; a second request is made beside the first only for a read that waits for bytes the first is not bringing, and a request nobody waits for stands still meanwhile. A reader is opened once the answer to its first request has come, and no longer when its first range is whole. On a host of this machine that gives 400 KiB a second and takes 0.1 s over a new connection, a reader was opened in 0.11 s where it took 0.27 s, and had its first 200 KiB 0.63 s after it was asked for where it took 1.01 s, over one connection where it took two. A read that failed is asked for again by the next read of its place, as before; what is read ahead is not asked for again and again while the server fails.
- **A video opened ahead and left paused takes its start and no more.** A media read two ranges ahead of every read from the moment it opened, so a neighbour of the video shown, opened to start at once, took megabytes from the connection of the one that played. Until it is first told to play a media now reads what opening asks for (one request of 256 KiB on Windows), and reads ahead from then on. See [a video opened ahead](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#a-video-opened-ahead).
- **A poster stays until the first frame is there.** A player given a source and told to play at once dropped its poster as it was told, and showed a black box until its video had opened and decoded a frame: the poster stays over it until then. Nothing of the picture is drawn under a poster, so one that does not cover the box (`PictureFit::Contain`) no longer shows the video's first frame at its sides. A media's picture is not drawn before its first frame, where it was a black frame of the video's size.

## 0.64.0 — 2026-11-20

Breaking:

- **An offset at the end of a scroll stays where it is when the content grows.** Every container at its bottom (or its right end) followed the end as the content grew, so a list that loads its next page in `on_scroll_reach_bottom` was carried down to the page that came, reached the bottom again and loaded the next, racing down by itself while the person sat at the end. It stays where it is now, as a page's and a `UIScrollView`'s does. A container that is to follow its end, a chat's or a log's list, says so: `ScrollState::set_sticks_to_end(true)` (see Added). A container the app sent to its end (`scroll_to_bottom_pending`, `stick_to_bottom_if_near_end`) stays there as it grows, until the person moves it away, as before. See [Scroll](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#scroll).

Added:

- `ScrollState::set_sticks_to_end(bool)` and `sticks_to_end()`: an offset at an end of the content follows that end as the content grows, while it is there. Off unless set.
- `Loader::pool_size(n)`: how many loads are read at once (on the loader's own threads, or as blocking tasks of the app's runtime) and how many HTTP agents the loader keeps; 4 unless told, as before. Told again, it is for the loads asked for from then on.
- `VideoPlayer::captions_button(bool)` and `settings_button(bool)`: an app leaves the captions' button (with their row in the menus and the key C) or the settings' button (with its menu) out, in every form. See [Video Player](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#video-player).
- **A media that plays round and round.** `Media::set_looped(true)`, `VideoPlayer::looped(true)` and `AudioPlayer::looped(true)`: at its end the media goes on from its start in what it has open, as a clip in a feed does. Nothing is opened again, an address read through the app's loader is asked for nothing it holds (a reader of an address now always holds its first megabyte), and it does not stop: `on_ended` is told each time round, `playing` stays true and its state does not say it ended. Setting `playing` again in `on_ended` stopped at the seam. On Windows it is a seek in the reader that is open; on macOS and iOS the system's player is sent to its start; a browser's `<audio>` is. See [Playing a video](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#playing-a-video).

- **The loader reads an address in parts.** `Loader::open` gives a `Reader` of an address, where it failed with `LoadError::Unsupported`: ranges of 1 MiB as they are read, two fetched ahead and sixteen kept, with the headers and the loader's timeout on each request. It asks for the first range before it returns, so a server that refuses fails it there with what it answered (`LoadError::Status(403)`, `NotFound`), and blocks: call it off the UI thread. A read that fails later gives an `io::Error` that `LoadError::from` turns into the same. What the cache on the disk keeps of the address is read from its file. It needs the feature `http`, and is not built in a browser. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).
- `Reader::content_type`: the type a server said its answer is (`video/mp4`), `None` by default.
- `Loader::headers(rule)`: headers of every request for an address, from the address: a picture's load, a sound's, and each request a video or a sound makes as it plays. A source's own (`Source::header`) come after and take the place of one of the same name.

Fixes:

- **A video waits only when it has nothing left to show.** On Windows a media at an address said it waited (`waiting`, `on_waiting_change`, a player's spinner) whenever the decoder read a part the loader had not fetched yet, and the decoder reads ahead of what plays: the spinner came and went over a video that went on playing. It waits now only where the media's thread has waited for its next sample, with nothing else to show or to give the output, for more than a quarter of a second while the part it needs is on the network: starved, and not reading ahead. A part that comes at once shows no spinner.
- **A video player has a captions' button only where it has captions.** `ui::VideoPlayer` drew a captions' button in every form (the touch form's top row, the large form's bar), a Captions row in its settings and its "more" menu, and C turned them on, for a video that has none. They are there now only where the player has captions: a track (`caption_cues`, `captions_track`), a line (`caption_text`), captions the app holds (`captions`, `on_captions_change`) or that are on. The settings' button is drawn only where its menu has something in it.
- A focused button pressed by Enter or Space stops at a handler of its `on_click` that stops the event at once (`stop_immediate_propagation`), as a click of the pointer does, and a submit button whose handler prevents the default sends no form: the keyboard called every handler and sent the form. So Ctrl+Enter (Cmd+Enter on a Mac) on a button with `on_open_aside` called its `on_click` too, and an app opened two tabs.
