- **A video or a sound at an address sends its headers.** A media opened from `Source::url(..).header(name, value)` (`Media::open`, `Media::sound`, `ui::VideoPlayer::source`, `ui::AudioPlayer::source`) sent none of them with what it asked of the server as it played: every video, and a sound larger than the loader reads whole. A host that answers 403 without its `Referer`, or one that wants an `Authorization`, could not be played ("This video can't be played"). On Windows the app's loader now reads every address a media plays (with the feature `http`) and Media Foundation only decodes: the headers, `allow`, the proxy, the timeout and the cache on the disk hold for it, seeking asks the server for the part it goes to, the media waits (`on_waiting_change`) while a part is on its way, and a server that refuses on the way fails it with what it answered (`MediaError::Server(403)`, where Media Foundation said "Access is denied"). On macOS and iOS the system's player is given the headers (`AVURLAssetHTTPHeaderFieldsKey`). A browser's `<audio>` cannot send them, and says so once in the log.
- **A large title goes to the way back as one title.** Pushing a screen over one whose large title was in view drew the title twice on its way to the new screen's way back: the large title shrinking and fading, and the way back's label growing and coming in, bold over regular a pixel or two apart, so that it looked smeared. Where the way back says the title, one text now goes between the two, shrinking and taking the way back's color as it goes, and the same when the screen is taken off, by a finger too. A way back that says "Back" for want of room still fades in as the title fades out.
- **A large title takes the colors of the bar given again.** A screen of a `StackOutlet` was not rendered again when the outlet was given a bar in other colors, so its large title (`StackOutlet::large_title_scroll`) kept the bar it was first built with: an app that built its bar in the light palette and again in the dark one when the system turned dark had a near-black large title on a dark screen. The colors a component reads in `create`, and in the root's first render, are the light palette's on a dark system, since the window hears the system's appearance after the root is mounted: the docs say so now ([The System's Settings](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#the-systems-settings)).
- **A phone emulated on a desktop keeps nothing for a status bar.** `Device::phone()` and `Device::tablet()` (and `LURQ_DEVICE`) gave the window the phone's safe area, 47 at the top and 34 at the bottom, so `ctx.safe_area()`, the navigation bar, the tab bar, sheets and the lightbox kept clear of a status bar and a home indicator a monitor does not have: an empty band over every screen. Their safe area is none now; the size, the coarse pointer and the mouse as fingers are as they were. `Device::with_safe_area(Device::PHONE_SAFE_AREA)` (or `TABLET_SAFE_AREA`), or `LURQ_DEVICE_INSETS=1` beside `LURQ_DEVICE`, gives the insets back, to see where a phone's would be. A phone keeps the system's own.
- **A finger that travels is no tap.** A finger that went more than 10 points from where it landed clicked what it landed on when it lifted there, where it had not scrolled anything: over a box where nothing scrolls, and after it rested into a long press, so a slow drag across a link in a list (a mouse that emulates fingers rests before it moves) opened the link. It clicks nothing now, in any direction; its `on_mouse_up` is still told, and a slider, a selection or a drag it holds ends as before. See [Touch](docs/src/content/docs/ios.md).
- **A finger shows no focus ring.** A finger that panned a list, or pulled it to refresh, left the ring the keyboard had shown on the focused control (a segmented control's chosen segment, a button of the bar): only a press counted as the pointer. A finger or a pen that lands takes the ring away now, as a mouse press does; Tab, the arrows and the keys show it, as before.
- **The loads of one host go over one connection.** Every load of an address made an HTTP agent of its own, so each picture paid a name lookup, a connection and a TLS handshake: an album of thirteen small thumbnails took seconds, and a picture's blurred placeholder waited in the same slow queue. The loader keeps its agents now (as many as `pool_size`) and lends them to its loads and to the readers of addresses a video plays from, so a load to a host a connection is open to goes over it. See [Loading](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#loading).

## 0.63.2 — 2026-11-20

Breaking:

- A `ui::Alert` has several actions: `action(..)` adds one after those there, where a second call took the place of the first, and `actions(..)` adds several (see Added). An alert given one action is as it was. A renderer of an alert reads `AlertView::actions`, a list (was `action`, one element or none), and builds their row with `view.actions()` (was `view.action()`).
- Enums of parts and states have variants more: `TagPart::Term`, `CalendarPart::Footer`, `ChoiceCardPart::Content`, `Reason` and `Lock`, `ChoiceCardState::Loading`, `CodeBlockState::CopyFailed`. A `match` over them with no wildcard must take them. `EditingWords` has a field more, `copy_failed`: one written out field by field must add it.

Added:

- **Pressables Tab passes by.** A `ui::Button`, `ui::ListItem` and `ui::Tag` (its remove button), and `ui::Tabs`, `ui::SegmentedControl` and `ui::Breadcrumb` for all their tabs, segments, links and buttons at once, take `tab_stop(false)`: Tab passes them by and a press of one leaves the focus where it was, in the field being typed in or nowhere, as many desktop apps keep Tab for their forms and reach their buttons, rows and tabs with the pointer and their own shortcuts. Such a control still takes the focus the app gives it (`Ctx::focus`, an agent over MCP), Enter and Space press it then, the arrows of tabs and segments move along them, and it is the same to what reads the tree. An element of the engine has the two halves apart: `tab_stop(false)` takes it out of Tab's order and keeps it focusable, and its focus group's arrows still reach it, which `tab_index(-1)` does not allow; `focus_on_press(false)` keeps a press from moving the focus. See [pointer-only pressables](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#pointer-only-pressables).
- **A press that opens beside what is open.** `on_open_aside(handler)` on a `ui::Button`, `ui::ListItem`, `ui::Tab` and `ui::BreadcrumbStep`, as a `ui::SidebarItem` has it: the middle button, or a press with Ctrl held (Cmd on macOS), and Enter with it while the control is focused, call the handler in place of the press, which does nothing else: no `on_click`, no tab selected, no step followed, no path gone to, no focus, no form submitted, and nothing around the control hears it. The handler is given the press, so an app opens a tab it switches to where Shift is held and one behind it where it is not. A middle press on such a control starts no scroll that follows the pointer.
- **Any component says a state of the app's to tooling.** `Styled::describe(name, value)`, which every builder of `lurq::ui` has, says it on the node of the component's root, where its id is, to what reads the tree (DevTools, the MCP tree, a test): `Callout::new(..).describe("tone", "warning")`, `Progress::new(0.4).describe("share", "40")`. It comes before what the component says of itself there, so a reader that takes the first value of a name (`a11y_name`) takes the app's, and a theme's renderer says it too. The instance's recipe carries it (`Recipe::describe`, `Recipe::descriptions`). As an element's `describe`, it says nothing without a tooling feature. See [the design system](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#one-instance-one-place-one-subtree).
- `Avatar::initials(&str)`: the letters the face shows, the app's in place of those lurq takes from the name, which stays what tooling calls the avatar. Empty letters show the icon of a person.
- **A spinner may move in steps, and stand still where motion is reduced.** A `SpinMotion` says how a spinning icon and a `Spinner` (its ring, dots and bars) move: `SpinMotion::SMOOTH`, as before, or `SpinMotion::stepped(per_second)`, each pose held until the next and no frame asked for between, the dots' and the bars' offsets put on the steps so that they change at the same moment; and `.still_when_reduced(true)`, which leaves them at rest where motion is reduced. `Icon::spin_motion` and `Spinner::motion` give one its own, and `Theme::set_spin_motion` (`UiDefinition::set_spin_motion`) every one given none, the ring of a loading button too. A spinner moves under reduced motion unless told otherwise. `SpinnerView` has a field more, `motion`. See [spin motion](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#spin-motion).
- **An alert holds several actions, at its end or under its words.** `ui::Alert::action` adds, `actions(..)` adds several, the least first and the main one last, 8 apart in the part `action`; `stacked(true)` (state `stacked`) puts them under the description and the body, at the words' start, wrapping where one line does not hold them, for words that need the alert's width or more actions than its end has room for. See [alert](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#alert).
- **A card is laid out as a column is, from its own builder.** `ui::Card` takes `padding`, `gap` and `align_items` for what is in it (its content and its body, two levels down), and `height`, `min_width`, `max_width`, `min_height`, `max_height`, `flex`, `flex_shrink`, `absolute` and `describe` for its surface, beside the `width` it had: `Card::new().padding(..).gap(..).child(..)` is laid out as a padded column is. The padding, the gap and the sizes are rules of the card over the theme's recipe, which a renderer reads in its parts. See [card](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#card).
- **A choice of its own: an id, a count, a state and a ref.** A `ui::Choice` takes `id(..)`, `count(n)`, `state(name, on)` and `element_ref(ElementRef)`, beside `disabled`: a `ui::SegmentedControl` and a `ui::RadioGroup` give the segment or the radio the id and name its parts after it (`<id>-label`, `<id>-count`), draw the count after the label (a part `count` of each, and `Radio::count`), resolve the state on that choice alone, and put the ref on what is pressed (`Radio::element_ref` too). A toggle group and a chip group take a choice's id and state. See [segmented control](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#segmented-control).
- **A tab disabled for a reason, and a badge.** `ui::Tab::disabled_reason(&str)` disables a tab and makes the reason its tooltip and its description to tooling; `Tab::badge(n)` draws a count of what waits for the person in a pill after the label (part `badge`, `<id>-badge`). A renderer gets the tab with its tooltip from `TabsView::tab_element`, and `TabEntry` has `badge` and `reason`. See [tabs](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tabs).
- **A dialog's mark, note and confirm.** `ui::Dialog::icon(name)` puts an icon in a well before the title, and `media(element)` the app's element there (parts `mark` and `icon`, state `mark`); `note(&str)` with `note_icon(name)` puts quiet words at the footer's start, the buttons at its end (parts `note` and `note_icon`, state `note`); `on_confirm(handler)` runs on Enter while the dialog is open, unless the focus is on a button, a link, a select or a text input of several lines, which keep Enter for themselves. The engine says that last in `KeyboardEvent::focus_takes_enter`, for any handler that gives Enter a meaning around a focused control. `DialogView` has `icon`, `media`, `note` and `note_icon`, and `mark()`, `note()` and `confirmer()`. See [dialog](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#dialog).
- **A field's error icon, and its control at the row's end.** `ui::Field::error_icon(name)` draws an icon before the description while the field is invalid (part `icon`), and `Field::control_end(true)` (state `control_end`) lays the label and the description on the left, taking the row's room (part `text`), and the control at its end, as a row of settings is. See [field](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#field).
- **A tooltip that wraps where it is told, has a note, is in mono, and shows only while a label is cut.** `ui::Tooltip::max_width(..)` is the widest its box is before its text wraps, over the recipe's 200; `note(&str)` puts a second line under the text (part `note`, state `note`), the text then its title in a medium weight; `mono(true)` (state `mono`) sets the text in the mono family; and `when_cut(&ElementRef)` shows it only while the text of that ref is cut, so that a label cut with an ellipsis says itself whole only where it is not whole already (and is not described by its tooltip while it says the same itself). A tooltip mounted on a bare text holds it in a row that gives way as the text would, so a label cut with an ellipsis is cut there too. `TooltipView` has a field more, `note`, and `view.note()`. See [tooltip](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#tooltip).
- **A text's ref says whether the layout cut it.** `ElementRef::text_cut()`: whether the element is a text the last layout drew in fewer letters than it has, by an ellipsis or a limit of lines.
- **An icon-only button has its label as its tooltip outside a toolbar.** `ui::Button::mount(ctx)` builds the button with its tooltip: an icon-only one with a label has its label, as a `Toolbar` gives the icons in it, and `Button::tooltip(Tooltip)` gives it one of the app's in place of that (a shortcut, a side, a tooltip for a button with words).
- **A menu item says more than its label.** `ui::MenuItem::detail(&str)` puts a second line under the label (part `detail`, state `detail`), the item as high as its two lines; `unavailable(reason)` makes it one that cannot be chosen (state `unavailable`, and `disabled`), with a lock at its end (part `lock`) and the reason under its label where it has no detail of its own, the reason read by tooling as what describes it; `leading(element)` stands an element of the app's before the label in place of the icon (part `leading`). `MenuItemView` has fields more, `leading`, `detail` and `reason`, and `view.leading()`, `view.detail()` and `view.lock()`. See [menu](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#menu).
- **A popover and a menu kept inside an element.** The engine's `Overlay::within(element)` and `Popup::within`, and `ui::Popover::within` and `ui::Menu::within`: where the collision strategy flips and clamps the panel, it does so inside that element's box, as it was last laid out, and then inside the window, which wins where they differ. A submenu given nothing of its own is kept where its menu is. See [popover](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#popover).
- **A status label.** `ui::StatusLabel`: an icon and a word in one tone (`StatusLabelVariant::Neutral`, `Muted`, `Info`, `Success`, `Warning`, `Destructive`), with no fill and no edge, the state of a run in a row of a table; `spin(true)` turns its icon as a spinner, by the theme's `SpinMotion` or its own (`spin_motion`). Two sizes, its words cut with an ellipsis where its room is short, a `status` to tooling. See [status label](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#status-label).
- **A radio card and a checkbox card hold more.** `content(element)` puts the app's elements under the description (part `content`), `mark(element)` an element of the app's in place of the icon, `disabled_reason(&str)` disables the card and says why under its words, to tooling (`a11y_disabled_reason`), with a lock in place of its mark or box (parts `reason`, `lock`), and `loading(bool)` holds it while what it chose is under way: no press, dimmed, its mark kept, busy to tooling (state `loading`). `ChoiceCardView` has `mark`, `content`, `reason`, `loading`, `take_icon()` and `take_content()`.
- **A code block copies what the app says, and says when the clipboard refused it.** `CodeBlock::copy_text(&str)` is what its button copies in place of the code shown (`on_copy` is told it). Mounted, a copy the clipboard does not take shows an alert in `Destructive`, named by `copy_failed_text` ("Couldn't copy", `EditingWords::copy_failed` in six languages), state `copy_failed`, for as long as Copied is shown; a check is shown only once the clipboard took the code. `CodeBlockView` has `copy_text`, `copy_failed_text` and `copy_failed`.
- `Calendar::footer(element)`: the app's element under the months, across the panel, 8 under a line (part `footer`, `CalendarView::footer`, `take_footer()`).
- `Tag::term(&str)`: a term before a tag's text, `branch:` before `main`, as the app writes it and quieter than the text (part `term`, `TagView::term`).
- `BreadcrumbStep::state(name, on)`: a state of the theme's recipe for one step alone, on its link or page and its icon: a key in mono, a last step drawn as an ancestor.
- **SVG icons in the theme's set.** `ThemeIcons::set_svg(name, IconSvg)` and `Theme::set_icon_svg` give a name an SVG in place of a glyph: `IconSvg::stroked` (its lines in the icon's color, as lucide's), `filled` (its shapes) or `colored` (its own colors, a brand's mark). An `Icon` of that name draws it at its size, in its color or the foreground of what it is in, turning where it spins; `ThemeIcons::svg` and `Theme::icon_svg` read it, `get` stays a glyph's. The engine paints an SVG in the foreground its node inherits where its data asks (`SvgData::fill_current_color`, `stroke_current_color`). With the feature `canvas`; without it such an icon keeps its room and draws nothing. See [icons](docs/src/content/docs/theme.md#icons).

## 0.63.1 — 2026-11-20

Breaking:

- The ids of a calendar's days and buttons follow the rule of the ids of parts (see Added): a `ui::Calendar`'s and a `ui::EventCalendar`'s day is `<id>-day-<date>` (was `<id>-<date>`), their buttons `<id>-nav-previous` and `<id>-nav-next` (were `-previous` and `-next`), and a `ui::MonthPicker`'s month and a `ui::YearPicker`'s year `<id>-cell-2026-11` and `<id>-cell-2026` (were `<id>-2026-11` and `<id>-2026`). The calendar of a date picker `due` is still `due-calendar`, so its days are `due-calendar-day-<date>`. A test or a tool that looked them up by id looks up the new ones.

Added:

- **A component's id names its parts.** A component of `lurq::ui` given `.id(x)` keeps `x` on its root and gives the node of each of its other parts `x-<part>`, the part's name as its recipe writes it, underscores and all (`status-dot`, `status-label`, `upload-fill`, `x-trigger_icon`), so `Tree::get_element_by_id`, a test, DevTools or an agent over MCP reaches a part with no id written for it. A part of which there are several is named by its key where it has one (a calendar's day `due-calendar-day-2026-11-20`, a weekday `x-weekday-mon`, its sides `x-nav-previous` and `x-nav-next`, an item, option, tab or segment by its key or value) and by its order where it has none (`about-term`, `about-term-2`). An item with an id keeps it and names its own parts after it; a component built inside another and given no id is named after what it stands for (a button's spinner `save-spinner`, a date picker's calendar `due-calendar`) and names its parts after that. The ids are the same at every render of the same content. A node that had an id keeps it, and a component given no id names nothing, so this breaks nothing but what counted on a node having no id: in a component given an id, nodes that had none now have one, which a lookup by id, a test that lists ids, and the matching of retained nodes among siblings see. A theme's renderer gets the same through what it gives `.part(..)`; `ResolvedRecipe::owned_by`, `owned_like`, `item`, `keyed_item`, `part_id` and `lend`, `ResolvedPart::keyed` and `unnamed`, and `Themed::ROOT` are what a component of an app's own uses to do as lurq's do. See [ids of parts](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#ids-of-parts).

## 0.63.0 — 2026-11-19

Breaking:

- A `ui::FileUpload` refuses a file of the same name and size as one it holds, or as one before it among those given at once, with `RejectReason::Duplicate` (new): it took it again, and stood it twice in the list. A `match` over `RejectReason` with no wildcard must add it.
- The `dx12` renderer's own shared video textures are removed: `Dx12VideoSurfaceAllocator`, `Dx12Nv12Surface`, `Dx12RenderEngine::with_video_surface_allocator`, `images::Dx12Nv12Image`, `images::Dx12Nv12ImageSlot`, `NativeImageData::from_dx12_nv12`, `from_dx12_nv12_texture` and `from_dx12_nv12_slot`, and `NativeImageBackend::Dx12Nv12`. An app gives the frames it decodes as `images::CardFrames` (`NativeImageData::from_card_frames`), which that renderer draws now as the `wgpu` renderer does: a surface is the handle of a shared texture, not an `ID3D12Resource` of the renderer's device, so the app opens nothing on it. A frame is told to be written by a fence (`CardFrames::with_fence`, `CardFrame::ready_at`); a keyed mutex on a texture is not taken, as the removed way took one.
- A touch screen's touches reach the tree as touches on Windows, Android, Linux and in the browser, as on iOS: on Windows a finger was the mouse (a tap clicked, with no `on_tap`, and the pointer stayed fine), and Windows' mouse copies of a touch are dropped. `MouseEvent::pointer` is `pointer_kind`, and `PointerKind` has `Pen` (a `match` with no `_` arm takes it); a pen is told on iOS, on Windows and in the browser. A finger's tap that a handler of `on_tap` takes, and a tap of two fingers or more, click nothing (they clicked), and two taps in a row are no `on_dblclick` (a finger's double tap is `on_double_tap`). See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#what-pressed).
- What a component says by itself is in the app's locale (see Added): in an app that sets one, the names of its buttons, its empty and loading lines and its counts are in that language. In English they are as they were, but for three: "1 of 1 step done" (was "steps"), a table filter of yes or no said "Yes" and "No" in its tag (were lower case), and a color picker's area is "Saturation and brightness" whatever its `saturation_label` and `brightness_label` unless they are told, when it is "<saturation>, <brightness>". `PasswordStrength::text` gives an `Arc<str>` (was `&'static str`). `FilterLabels::several` is a `core::Plural` (a `"{count} chosen".into()` still converts). The bottom tab bar's `TabBar::more_title` and the stack's `NavigationBar::back` are empty unless the app sets them, for the locale's word. `ApplicationMenu::default()` is in the locale of the render it is made in, English outside one.
- The caret of a text input and the line under what an input method composes are the theme's, and the design system's theme draws the design's: a caret 1.5 wide and nine sevenths of the font size high (18 in type of 14) where it was 1 wide and as high as the font size, and a line of 1.5 where it was 1. A single-line input scrolls 1.5 past the end of its text for it, and a multiline input whose lines do not wrap is half a pixel wider. `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` and `bare()` keep the caret of 0.62, and `theme.set_caret(ThemeCaret::legacy())` brings it to any theme. `ThemeCaret::new(mode)` and `ThemeCaret::default()` are the design's caret, so an app on the legacy theme that set one sets `ThemeCaret::legacy().with_mode(mode)` or calls `Theme::set_caret_mode`; `ThemeCaret` is no longer `Eq`. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#the-caret).
- A styled input has an underline mark and kinds of paragraphs, and five types gained for them: `SpanStyle` a field `underline`, `SelectionStyle` a field `underline`, `ThemeTextMarks` a field `underline` (one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..Default::default()`), `TextMark` a variant `Underline` (`TextMark::ALL` is five long) and `StyledEdit` a variant `Paragraphs { range }` (a `match` on them with no wildcard must take them).
- A child of a container with `presence()` added again, by its key or its id, while it leaves ends its leaving and is the one added, the same element with its state. It was a new one beside the one leaving until that one's exit ended, so a panel opened again while its rows faded out (an `Accordion`'s, a `Collapsible`'s, a `PropertyGrid`'s category's) held its rows twice and was twice as high, and a `Banner` shown again while it faded out stood twice in the page. What wants the old way gives the one coming back a key of its own each time (`.key(format!("saved-{shown}"))`), as `Toasts::show` does. A layer opened again while it leaves does the same: see the line under Fixes. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#children-of-a-container).
- `ScrollBarStyle` has three fields more, `thumb_inset`, `thumb_hovered_color` and `thumb_pressed_color`: a style written out field by field must add them. The design system's theme draws the design's bar: a thumb of 6 inside a lane of 8, round, `MutedForeground` under the pointer and while it is dragged, where its thumb was as wide as its lane and the same under the pointer; each bar of a container that scrolls both ways has its own, and where both are there each track ends before the other's lane. `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` keeps the bar of 0.61, `ScrollBarStyle::default()`. See [scroll](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#scroll).
- `ShrinkLimit` has a variant more, `ContentFirst`: a child that keeps to its content minimum while the children of its order can take the overflow, then gives way below it to its `min_width`, the last first. A `match` over `ShrinkLimit` with no wildcard must add it. The content minimum of a line on the other axis (a `Column` in a row) is the widest of its children's, a child with a `min_width` of its own counted at it, where it was the column's natural width. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#give-way-order-and-limits).
- A growing child with a flex basis in a `Row` or a `Column` is its basis and its share of the room the bases leave, as on the web: the free room of a line is its length less the gaps, the children sized on their own and the bases. The bases were left in the free room, so a child of `flex_full(1.0, 0.0, Some(50.0))` beside a child of 50 in a row of 200 was 200 wide and ran out of the row, where it is now 150; one that could shrink shrank back by its basis. A basis of 0, the common `flex_full(1.0, 1.0, Some(0.0))`, lays out as before. See [layout](docs/src/content/docs/layout.md#flex).
- A value that changes because the theme does, a color of the other appearance, is applied at once to an element that has a transition for it, as it is to everything else in the window. Such an element used to fade from its old color while the window around it had already changed: a button stayed light for its transition's length on a window that had turned dark. A transition is for what the element does itself: a state, a style set on it.
- **The default theme is the design system's.** `Theme::new()` has the design's palette (`pencil/lurq-design-system.pen`) in place of the blue one of 0.61: a neutral primary, other borders, a near-black foreground. It has a dark palette too and follows the system's appearance, so on a system in its dark look a window is dark: text that names no color is near-white, and a window whose root paints no background is cleared to the dark `Background`. The radii are the design's too: `Sm` 4, `Md` 6, `Lg` 8, where they were 3, 5 and 6. So is the type scale: body text is 14 where it was 16, so `Text::new(...)` with no style is smaller, headings are semibold with tighter letter spacing, and `TypographyStyle::Mono` is 13 on lines of 1.3, the line its family draws, where it was 16 on lines of 1.2. An app that paints light backgrounds of its own under text that names no color must say what it wants: `app.theme().apply(ThemeDefinition::legacy())` brings the whole 0.61 look back, with one palette, and `app.theme().set_preferred_appearance(Some(Appearance::Light))` keeps the new look in its light palette. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md).
- `ThemeTypography` has two fields more, `sans` and `mono_family`: one written out field by field must add them.
- `TypographyStyle` has eight variants more, `Heading1` to `Heading6`, `Prose` and `Article`, and `ThemeTypography` a field for each: a `match` on the one must take them, and the other written out field by field must add them, or end with `..ThemeTypography::default()`. `TextStyle` has a field more, `case`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..TextStyle::default()`.
- **Markdown is on the theme's roles.** `ThemeMarkdown`'s colors are `TextColor`s, a palette role or a color: `MarkdownTextStyle::color`, `MarkdownBlockStyle::background` and `border_color`, `MarkdownLinkState`'s four colors and `MarkdownFocusRing::color`. Write `Some(PaletteColor::Info.into())` for a role, `Some(Color::from_hex("#2563eb").into())` for a color. A document is drawn in the theme it is in, light and dark; its default look is the design's (`pencil/lurq-design-system.pen`, Markdown), no longer dark code blocks and slate grays. `MarkdownLinkState` and `MarkdownFocusRing` are `Clone` and no longer `Copy`. `TextStyle` has a field more, `baseline_shift`, and `MarkdownTextStyle` one, `baseline_shift`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..Default::default()`. `ThemeMarkdown` has fields more (`task_item_spacing`, `bullet_gap`, `box_gap`, `bullet_indent`, `bullet_size`, `table_stripe`, `heading_2_rule`, `kbd`, `task_done`, `rule`, `image_box`, `image_height`, `image_caption`, `details_box`, `details_summary_box`, `details_summary`, `details_padding`, `footnote_reference`, `footnote`, `footnotes_rule_width`): one written out field by field must add them, or end with `..ThemeMarkdown::default()`.
- A Markdown code block is a `ui::CodeBlock` and a quote a `ui::Blockquote`, one look with those wherever they are: the code under a bar named by its language, with the button that copies it, the quote's words 14 in italics in the muted foreground after a line of 2. `ThemeMarkdown` has no fields of their own any more: `code_block`, `code_block_label`, `code_block_spacing`, `code_block_box`, `code_block_scrollbar`, `blockquote_box`, `blockquote_marker` and `blockquote_gap` are gone. Style the theme's `code_block` and `blockquote` recipes in their place (`theme.patch::<CodeBlock>(|recipe| recipe.base(CodeBlockPart::Root, ..))`, `BlockquotePart::Label` for a quote's words). A code block's lines are where the code block puts them, under its bar: an element lookup that counted on the old box sees the code block's parts. A selection across a document copies a code block's language and its lines, as before, and none of the glyphs of the document's controls.
- `MarkdownProps::on_link_click` is `on_link_select`, by the design system's rule for what a document of links tells: which one was followed. `on_link_activate` is as it was.
- A Markdown document with no style of its own is in the theme's `TypographyStyle::Article` (15 on lines of 1.65), its headings in `Heading1` to `Heading6`, where it was in `TextStyle::default()` with headings scaled from it.
- `MarkdownBlock` has two variants more, `Admonition` and `Details`, and `MarkdownInline` one, `Kbd`: a `match` on them must take them. A quote that starts `> [!NOTE]` (and the four others GitHub writes) is an `Admonition`, `<details>` and `<summary>` HTML blocks around Markdown are a `Details`, and `<kbd>` in a sentence a `Kbd`, where they were a quote and HTML.
- A Markdown footnote is drawn at the end of the document, numbered by the first reference to it, and a reference is its number: `rendered_text()` has `1` where it had `[^1]`. A bulleted item's marker is a dot (a selection still copies its `-`), and a task's `[x]` is a checkbox, no longer text.
- `TextHighlight` has two fields more, `padding` and `border`: one written out field by field must add them, or be made with `TextHighlight::new`.
- `ThemePalette` has a field for each of the design's roles (43 in place of 23). A palette written out field by field with no `..ThemePalette::default()` must add them; build one from `ThemePalette::light()`, `dark()` or `legacy()` instead.
- `Theme::set_palette_color(role, color)` sets the role in the light palette and in the dark one, so a color set once holds when the system changes its appearance; `Theme::set_palette(palette)` sets the palette of the appearance the theme is in and keeps it when the appearance changes and comes back. On a theme with a palette for each appearance both were lost at the next change of appearance.
- A window whose root paints no background is cleared to the theme's `Background`, which is white in the light palette as before and near-black in the dark one. It was always white.
- A text built with `Text::styled(..)` from a style that keeps the default text color names no color, and takes the `.foreground(..)` of an element above it, as a text with no style does. It took the theme's foreground whatever was above it.
- A notch of a mouse's wheel is animated (see Added), so the content is where the notch sent it about 150 ms later and not in the next frame. What could rely on the old way: an `on_scroll` handler also hears an event with a zero delta for every step of the animation, where it heard the wheel's event alone; `on_scroll_reach_top` and `on_scroll_reach_bottom` run when the content gets there; `Tree::has_active_timeline()` is true while it is on its way; and a harness that sends `SyntheticInput::wheel` or the MCP server's `wheel` and reads the offset at once reads it on its way, so it waits for the window to be idle (`lurq_wait`) first. `Tree::scroll`, a touchpad and a finger scroll at once as before, and `app.theme().set_smooth_scroll(false)` brings the old way back for the wheel.

- A disabled element hears the pointer come and go: its `on_mouse_enter` and `on_mouse_leave` run as the pointer comes onto it and leaves it, so a tooltip can say why it is disabled. They did not run. Nothing else of the pointer reaches it: it is not hovered, and no press, click or drop reaches it.
- A modal over the whole window (`Modal::target(ModalTarget::Root)`) in a window with a `WindowChrome` covers the window under the title bar, where it covered all of it with its top under the bar: a dialog is centred in what is seen, and a veil ends at the bar. What is passed to `WindowChrome::overlay` still has all of the window.
- `BackgroundColor` has a variant more, `CurrentForeground`: a `match` on it must take it.
- `AnimatableValue` has two variants more, `Percent` and `Dash`, and `AnimatableProperty` one, `BorderDash`: a `match` on them must take them.
- `AnimatableValue` has a variant more, `Fit { px, content }`: a `match` on it must take it.
- `StrokeStyle` has a field more, `side_colors`: one written out field by field must add it, or end with `..StrokeStyle::default()`.
- `EventHandlers` has a field more, `press_repeats`, and `SelectTriggerState` one, `input`: one written out field by field must add it.
- A single-select whose checkmark is after the label keeps the checkmark's room on the selected row only: the labels and trailing elements of the other rows run to the row's end. Every row kept an empty slot of the checkmark's width.
- `FormValue` has a variant more, `Range(f64, f64)`: a `match` on it must take it.
- **The form controls are the design system's components** (decision D7): `FormTextInput` is a `ui::Field` holding a `ui::Input` (a `ui::Textarea` where it is multiline), `FormCheckboxInput` a `ui::Checkbox` with its label beside the box, `FormSliderInput` and `FormRangeSliderInput` a field holding a `ui::Slider` or `ui::RangeSlider`, `FormPrimaryButton` a primary `ui::Button` and `FormSecondaryButton` an outline one, `FormControlField` a `ui::Field`. They compile as they were written, and draw the design's look from those components' recipes: `FormTheme` styles nothing (see Deprecated), an error is the box's edge and no `DangerMuted` fill, the focus is the theme's ring, the secondary button is an outline one, the checkbox's label is beside it and not above. A control made `disabled(true)`, or one of a form that is submitting, takes no input (a text field took typing before), and one made `required(true)` has the field's `*`. The checkbox is no engine `Checkbox` node; it gives the form `on` while it is checked, as that did. Under `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` the buttons, the checked box, the error and the focus keep 0.62's colors. See the migration guide, "The Form Controls".

- `DragEvent` has a field more, `outside_window`: one written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `lurq::app::devtools::load_fonts` is gone: DevTools draws its icons with the theme's and loads no font of its own. Take the call out of the app.
- With `devtools`, `ComponentContextDebug` is no longer made of two fields: it has `reactive` too, and `value()` and `history()`. `ctx.provide`, `ctx.provide_if_changed` and `ctx.create_context` take any type, as they did.
- `PersistentStorageSnapshotEntry` has a field more, `fields`: the fields of a stored struct, each with its value as it is written, empty for any other value. One written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `ComponentValueChangeDebug` has a field more, `sequence`: which change it is among all the changes of every signal and memo, a later one a greater number. One written out field by field must add it.
- With `devtools`, `DevToolsNode` has a field more, `render` (how the component rendered, `None` on a node no component rendered), and `DevToolsProps` one, `system` (the system's settings as the inspected window knows them): one written out field by field must add them.
- lurq has default features, the desktop set: `winit`, `wgpu`, `clipboard`, `file_dialog`, `image`, `canvas`, `form`, `router` and `query`. An app that named no feature gets them; one that leaves part of the set out writes `default-features = false` and its list, and a browser app does, since the system clipboard's crate does not build for a browser. See [Features](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#features).
- The feature `svg` is gone: SVG comes with `canvas`, which takes `usvg`. `cfg(feature = "svg")` in an app's code is `canvas`. `raster` and `render` are lurq's own, turned on by `image`, `canvas`, `media` and the renderers; an app does not name them.
- The feature `i18n` is gone: `I18n`, `App::i18n` and `Ctx::t` with the rest are in every build. Translations read from JSON still need `serde`.
- The feature `resources` is gone: it was the old name of `http`.
- An SVG is always rasterized at the size it is drawn. A build with `svg` and without `raster` tessellated it into triangles, sharp under a GPU transform or a zoom; that path is gone with its shaders, and `RenderList` has no field `svgs` (a `RenderList` written out field by field drops the line). See [Features](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#svg-is-canvas).
- `screenshot` turns on the `image` feature (it took the `image` crate alone), so a build with `screenshot`, `devtools` or `mcp` has the decoders and `ui::Picture`'s loading.
- `WgpuExternalImageSlot::set_texture_view` returns `Result<u64, WgpuTextureError>` (was `u64`): a texture lurq cannot sample (no `TEXTURE_BINDING`, not 2D, multisampled, or an integer, depth or 32-bit float format) is refused with a line in the log, and the slot keeps the view it had, where it drew nothing and said nothing. A call whose value was used writes `?` or `.expect(..)`; one that dropped it drops the `Result` with `let _ =`. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#drawing-with-your-own-gpu-code).
- `AppEvent` has a variant more, `GraphicsDeviceLost` (see Added): a `match` on it with no `_` arm must take it.
- `SharedWgpuContext` is no longer written out field by field: it has a field of lurq's own, which says which device it is of. An extension is given it, as before.
- A `GpuViewport` given no size is 300 by 150 logical pixels, as a `Canvas` is, where it was one pixel square, and one given a width or a height alone keeps the other at 150 or 300, where it kept the square's shape. The `wgpu` engines of an app's windows share one device (see Added), where each window's engine had one of its own: a view of the app's is drawn in every window, and an engine that must keep a device apart is made `with_own_device()`. lurq's DevTools window no longer runs the app's frame extensions, which it initialized and prepared as a window of the app's.

Deprecated:

- The palette roles of 0.61, each in favor of one of the design's: `Accent` → `Primary`, `AccentHover` → `PrimaryHover`, `AccentMuted` → `Highlight`, `SurfaceBase` and `SurfaceInput` → `Background`, `SurfacePanel` → `Canvas`, `SurfaceRaised` → `Card`, `BorderStrong` → `Input`, `BorderFocus` → `Ring`, `TextPrimary` → `Foreground`, `TextSecondary` and `TextMuted` → `MutedForeground`, `TextInverse` → `PrimaryForeground`, `Danger` → `Destructive`, `DangerMuted` → `DestructiveMuted`. The `PaletteColor` variants and the `ThemePalette` fields still compile, with a warning that names the replacement. Each keeps a color of its own, and setting a role also sets the deprecated roles it replaces, so `Select`, the form controls and a checkbox or slider with no style of its own, which still read them, follow a theme set through the new roles. `PaletteColor::replacement()` gives the role that replaces one.
- `FormTheme` and its parts (`FormFieldTheme`, `FormInputTheme`, `FormCheckboxStyle`, `FormSliderStyle`, `FormButtonTheme`, `FormButtonRole`, `FormTextRole`), `Theme::form`, `Theme::set_form` and `ThemeDefinition::form`, in favor of the recipes of `ui::Field`, `ui::Input`, `ui::Textarea`, `ui::Checkbox`, `ui::Slider` and `ui::Button` (`Theme::patch_recipe`), which the form controls are drawn by. Nothing reads them.
- `components::Checkbox`, the engine's checkbox node, in favor of `ui::Checkbox`, the design's, with its label, an indeterminate and an invalid state and a recipe in the theme; and `components::ActivityIndicator`, the platform's spokes, in favor of `ui::Spinner`. Both compile with a warning; the form's checkbox and the pull to refresh still draw them. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#components-that-moved-to-lurqui).
- The phone's pieces take their colors from the theme through a recipe, in the design's roles, light or dark as the theme is: `SheetStyle` (`Background`, a grabber of `Input`, `Scrim` behind at 1 where it was white over black at 0.3), `SheetHostStyle`, `TabBar` (`Background` over `Border`, `Foreground` for the active tab and `MutedForeground` for the others, a `Destructive` badge, where it was translucent white with `#007AFF`), `NavigationBar` (`Background`, `Border`, `Foreground` for the title and the way back), `PullToRefreshStyle` and `ActivityIndicatorProps` (`MutedForeground`) and `SwipeAction` (`Background` on `MutedForeground`, `DestructiveForeground` on `Destructive`). Each keeps its recipe under a name of its own (`phone_sheet`, `phone_sheet_host`, `phone_tab_bar`, `phone_navigation_bar`, `phone_pull_to_refresh`, `phone_swipe_row`) with parts an app patches (`SheetNode`, `SheetHostPart`, `TabBarPart`, `NavigationBarPart`, `PullToRefreshPart`, `SwipeRowPart`). Their `dark()` and `for_appearance(..)` are deprecated and give 0.62's colors; `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` gives 0.62's light ones (`UiDefinition::legacy()`).
- The unstyled checkbox and slider nodes fill their box, track and thumb as the recipes `engine_checkbox` and `engine_slider` say (by `ui::CheckboxPart`/`CheckboxState` and `ui::SliderPart`): `Background`, `Primary` checked, `Border` and `Primary`, where the engine painted the deprecated `SurfaceInput` and `Accent`; the look is the same in the design's palettes and `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` keeps the old roles. A `StackOutlet`'s shades (under a moving screen, behind the list of the screens under) are the theme's `Scrim` through the recipe `phone_stack` (`StackPart`), where they were black; the legacy theme keeps black.
- `SelectStyle::new()` takes its colors from the recipe the theme keeps for the engine's select (`engine_select`, by the parts and states of `ui::Select`), in the design's roles where it named the deprecated ones; the look is the same in the design's palettes, and `ThemeDefinition::legacy()` keeps the roles of 0.61. An app that colored its select by setting a deprecated role sets the new one or patches the recipe.
- The engine's unstyled `Button`, `TextInput`, `Link`, `Popup`, `Select`, `Slider`, `Sheet`, `Video` and `WindowChrome` say in their docs that they are what the design system builds on, and name the `ui` component an app normally wants.
- The engine's custom title bar is drawn in the theme's roles: `ChromeTitleBar::new()` is `Muted` under a line of `Border` (it was `#101215` under `#252a32`), `ChromeBorderPolicy::PlatformDefault` paints `Border`, and `WindowControls`' controls are `Foreground` on `SecondaryHover` with the close one on `Destructive` (`DestructiveHover` held) and its glyph `DestructiveForeground` (they were `#d9dee7` on `#232934` and `#2d3440`, close `#c0392b` and `#922b21`). An app that wants the old dark bar on every theme writes it: `ChromeTitleBar::new().background("#101215").border_bottom(Border::inside(1.0, Color::from_hex("#252a32")))` and `WindowControls::new().colors(WindowControlColors { foreground: Color::from_hex("#d9dee7").into(), .. })`.
- Which keys are undo, redo, cut, copy, paste and select all is one function's, `KeyboardEvent::edit_command()` (see Added), and a text input, a list, a tree view, a table and a data grid take them through it. So Cmd+Y no longer redoes on a Mac (Cmd+Shift+Z does), Ctrl+Shift+A no longer selects all in a text input, and Ctrl+Alt with a letter (AltGr on Windows, which types a letter) is no command, where Ctrl+Alt+Z undid and AltGr+C copied. A copy whose `on_copy` handlers set no text copies the text selected on the page, as a copy with no `on_copy` handler does, where it wrote nothing. Select all in a listbox, a sortable list, a tree view, a table and a data grid is their `on_select_all`, which an `on_key_down` handler that prevents Ctrl+A still keeps from running. See [Editing commands](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#editing-commands-undo-redo-copy-cut-paste).
- The mouse's side buttons are `MouseButton::Back` and `MouseButton::Forward`, where they were `MouseButton::Other(4)` and `Other(5)`: a `match` with no `_` arm takes them. With the rest of going back (see Added) they move a mounted router by themselves, as Alt+← and Alt+→ (Cmd+[ and Cmd+] on Apple's), a keyboard's Back key, Android's back, a trackpad's swipe and a finger from the edge do: `on_history_swipe`'s default is the command, so a handler that moved the router itself moves it twice (take it away, or `prevent_default` where it does something else). Alt+← and Alt+→ are no other key once they are the command, in a text input too. A finger from the left or the right edge is the gesture wherever a router is mounted. See the [migration guide](docs/src/content/docs/migration-v0-63.md#back-and-forward).

Added:

- `App::set_audio_category` and `media::AudioCategory`: whether the app's sound is heard with an iPhone's ring switch on silent. `System` (the default) is not, as a game's or an interface's sounds; `Playback` is, as a player of music, podcasts or video. The gallery sets `Playback`
- **The browser is a supported platform** (Chrome, Edge, Firefox, Safari; WebGPU, or WebGL2 with `webgl`). In a page: `Media::sound` and `ui::AudioPlayer::source` play in the page's `<audio>`, with the media's events; the app's `Loader` loads files, resources and addresses with the page's `fetch`, and `Loader::proxy` (every platform) gives the address to fetch another server's through; a picture is decoded by the browser off the page's thread (`createImageBitmap`); a file picked comes with its bytes (`DroppedFile::read`, `with_bytes`, on every platform), `FileDialog::save` downloads in a page and writes where the person says elsewhere, and files dragged onto the page reach `on_file_drop`; composed text (an input method, dead keys, a phone's keyboard, dictation) reaches text inputs through a textarea of the shell's own; a picture crosses the clipboard as a PNG both ways; `PersistentStorage` keeps its values in `localStorage`; the page marks its first frame (`lurq:first-frame`). The workspace's `web` profile builds a page for its size: the gallery is 25 MB, 8.6 MB gzipped (42 MB before). See [Web](docs/src/content/docs/web.md), and `design/browser-checks.md` for what to check by hand in each browser.
- The gallery's page ships fonts it may publish (Inter and JetBrains Mono, SIL Open Font License), draws the GPU Viewport page's rings, keeps the Persistent Storage page's values and plays the Audio Player page's sources in a browser. A page's View source opens its file on GitHub, on master, on every system, where it opened the file of the checkout it was built from in an editor; Open in Pencil is gone.
- **A media's events, as the web has them.** `lurq::media::Media` tells `on_load(|info: &MediaInfo|)` (it opened: its length, its picture's size, whether it has sound), `on_error(|e: &MediaError|)`, `on_ended()`, `on_playing_change(bool)` and `on_waiting_change(bool)`, derived from its state: on the UI thread (the media's thread wakes the loops of the thread that listens, and a window's next turn tells them), once each time, in the order it lives them, with no polling of the app's. `ui::VideoPlayer` and `ui::AudioPlayer` tell the same of their `source` (`on_playing_change`, which heard a press, hears the media stop and start by itself too: at its end, a track that repeats); a player without a source tells none of them, its values being the app's. `core::MediaInfo` is new. See [media events](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#media-events).
- `ui::Picture::on_error` is told a kind, `core::PictureError` (`ui::PictureError`): `NotFound`, `Denied`, `TimedOut`, `TooLarge`, `Server(status)`, `Network`, `NotAPicture`, `Unavailable`, `Other`, with `detail()` for what the system or the decoder said; `MediaWords::picture_error` says it in the locale's words (three words more: `not_a_picture`, `picture_unavailable`, `picture_failed`). It was told a sentence (`Fn(&str)`). `images::ImageDecodeError::kind` gives the same kind.
- **A frame callback for an app: `ctx.on_frame`**, lurq's `requestAnimationFrame`. `ctx.on_frame(|frame| ..)` runs a callback once in each frame the window draws, in step with the display, for as long as it returns `true`: for a spring, a body with physics, a drag's inertia, a game or a simulation on a canvas. `Frame` has the frame's `time` on the runtime's clock and the `delta` since the frame the callback last ran for (zero on the first; `delta_secs()`). It runs at the start of the frame, before the window is rendered and laid out, so a signal it sets is in that frame. The `FrameCallback` it returns has `cancel()`, `start()` and `is_active()`; the callback is dropped with its component, and `WindowHandle::on_frame` is the same for code outside one. While a callback runs the window draws a frame each refresh, paced as a continuous animation is (`Tree::next_timeline_change()`, `Tree::has_frame_callbacks()`), each laid out and painted, beside a playing video too; with none nothing is drawn. Paused while the window is minimized or occluded, with a delta of zero on the first frame after; reduced motion does not stop it. In a test it runs on `Tree::set_animation_clock`. The gallery's Animation page has a ball on a spring that follows the pointer. See [animation](docs/src/content/docs/animation-transforms.md#your-own-frames).
- **The gallery shows how to style.** A Styling page in Getting started (`/styling`): a palette role changed, a recipe patched, `.style`, `.rules` with a selector, `.state` and `.force` on one instance, the order they are laid in, a part's renderer of its own, each live with its code. Every component's playground has a Styling tab beside its props: the parts and states it declares, a part restyled live for the one on the stage or for every one in the stage's own theme, and the code of both. For it, `Themed::parts()` and `Themed::states()` (a `Declared`: the names and the Rust that writes them, from each component's `*Part` and `*State` enums, `names!`'s `declared()`), the `Styled` trait every builder of `lurq::ui` implements (`rules`, `state`, `style`), `Theme::reset_recipe`, `reset_renderer` and `reset_part_renderer`, and `Selector::held_states` and `interaction`.
- **Back and forward in one place**: `NavigationCommand::Back` and `Forward`, as `EditCommand` is for editing. Its sources are the mouse's side buttons on their release, Alt+← and Alt+→ (Cmd+[ and Cmd+] on Apple's platforms, read by the bracket or by its place), a keyboard's Back and Forward keys and Android's back (`KeyboardEvent::navigation_command()`), a trackpad's swipe between pages and a finger from the edge (`on_history_swipe`'s default), a browser's back and forward for the router that follows the address (a `popstate` to the entry beside), and the app: `Ctx::history_back`, `history_forward` and `history_runner` (`HistoryRunner::back`, `forward`, `run`, `run_in`), `WindowHandle::history_back`, `history_forward` and `run_navigation`, MCP's `lurq_interact` actions `history_back` and `history_forward` and its buttons `back` and `forward`, and the phone emulator, whose side buttons are the phone's back. Element events `on_history_back` and `on_history_forward` (and `off_*`) are told a `HistoryEvent` (`command`, `source`, `pass_on`), from the focused element (from under the pointer for a pointer's source, from the last element that hears it with nothing focused) out to the root; a router mounted on the way takes it after its element's handlers, and with nothing taking it the routers elsewhere in the window, the innermost first. A dialog closes on going back instead of leaving the page by handling it. A `StackOutlet`'s way back and its finger from the edge go back through it from the top screen, which may take it (a screen with something unsaved), and a dragged screen comes back. `NavigationCommand::accelerator()` gives the keys to show. See [Going back and forward](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#going-back-and-forward) and [routing](docs/src/content/docs/routing.md#going-back-and-forward).

- The gallery's **Engine** group: a page for each of the engine's primitives, built as a component's page is (a playground whose controls are the props, then sections with the code of what they show): layout, sizing, position, grid, sticky, scroll, virtualized list, zoom scroll, visual, borders, transform, style, theme, images, SVG, canvas, GPU viewport, animation, presence, text, rich text, text input, glyph atlas, events, reactivity, components, context, persistent storage, drag and drop, the router and outlets, the engine's own controls and overlays, at `/engine/…`. It replaces the demo: `crates/demo` is removed, and what its binary did besides its pages moved, `--renderer dx12` and `--profile-log` (built with `--features perf_profile`) to the gallery, the window of the lifecycle and menus to `cargo run -p lurq --example lifecycle --features mcp`, and the continuous video probe to `cargo run -p lurq --example video_probe`. See [Getting Started](docs/src/content/docs/getting-started.md#run-the-gallery).
- Every gesture of a phone, through one path: `Tree::finger(id, phase, x, y, force)` gives a finger to the tree as a touch screen reports it (its own events, the pointer it stands for, the gestures), `Tree::finger_tick` runs what time brings (a long press, momentum, a list springing back) and `Tree::finger_deadline` says when; the winit shell, the emulator and MCP all go through it. New gestures: a double tap held and dragged zooms with one finger (`on_pinch`, down to zoom in); three fingers swiped left undo and right redo, pinched in copy and out paste, on the focus as their keys would (`EditCommand`), and tapped open the menu of editing commands, a `ContextMenu` bar lurq shows over the app by itself in the locale's words (`Window::edit_menu`, `EditMenuRequest`; `EditingWords` has `cut`, `copy`, `paste`). In text by touch (not on iOS, which draws its own): a long press puts the caret under the finger with a loupe and moves it with the finger, a selection has two handles to drag while the pointer is coarse, and the menu opens over what a finger selected. A pen's hover hovers: `Tree::pen_hover`, from a pen in range over a Windows screen and an Apple Pencil over an iPad. Two fingers' pinch and rotation are measured once both fingers of a frame moved, and a third finger ends them. See [every gesture](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#every-gesture).
- A phone on a desktop: `App::emulate(Device::phone())` (or `Device::tablet()`), `LURQ_DEVICE=phone|tablet` for any app, `--device phone` in the gallery. The window takes the device's size, its safe area reaches `ctx.safe_area()`, the pointer is pinned coarse, and the mouse is fingers through `Tree::finger`: a click a tap, a drag a pan or a swipe, a held press a long press, Alt+drag a pinch and a rotation, Alt+Shift+drag two fingers, Alt+Shift+Ctrl+drag three and Alt+Shift+Ctrl with the wheel a three-finger pinch, drawn as dots while a modifier is held; nothing hovers, the wheel scrolls. See [a phone on a desktop](docs/src/content/docs/app-runtime.md#a-phone-on-a-desktop).
- A finger reaches what answered only a double click: `ui::Splitter` restores a pane's size and `ui::DocumentTabs` keeps a preview at a double tap of one finger, and `ui::Table` and `ui::TreeView` in the touch form open a row at a single tap, never held back for a second.
- MCP's `lurq_touch`: tap (`fingers`, `count`), double tap, long press, pan, swipe (`direction`, `speed`), edge pan, pinch (`scale`), rotate (`angle`), one-finger zoom, three-finger swipe and pinch, played through `Tree::finger` on a clock of their own. `lurq_inspect` with a `query` or a `role` searches at any depth. See [touch gestures](docs/src/content/docs/mcp.md#touch-gestures).
- `ui::LinkPreview`: what a link says about itself, shown before it is followed, as the board draws it: a card of 360 (the picture over the site, the title and a line of the description), a compact row of 432 and an inline link of 24 that stands in a sentence (`LinkPreviewVariant`), each one link that opens its address (`on_open` to decide), with its skeleton while the page is read, the text alone where there is no picture, the play mark and the length of a video, the address and the link mark where the page tells nothing, a lift and an underlined title under the pointer, and a close of its own in a composer (`on_remove`). It works from the address alone, and every field the app gives (`title`, `description`, `site`, `image`, `icon`, `video`, `no_image`, `no_icon`, `no_site`) is shown over what is fetched; where everything shown is given nothing is fetched. Its words are `Locale::display`'s. See [Link Preview](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#link-preview).
- `resources::LinkMeta::from_html`, a scanner of a page's head of lurq's own (`<title>`, OpenGraph, Twitter Card, the description, the icons, `<base>`), tolerant of bad markup and reading UTF-8 and the charsets a meta names; `LinkFetcher`, `LinkFetch`, `LinkRequest`, `LinkPreviewError`; `PageFetcher`, lurq's own, which reads the start of a page through the app's loader (feature `http`) and keeps what it says in memory by the address; `set_link_fetcher` for an app's own. See [link previews](docs/src/content/docs/resources-media.md#link-previews).
- `Loader::load_start(source, bytes)`: the first bytes of a source, and no error where there is more.
- `Ctx::focus_handle()`, a `FocusHandle` for a handler: `focus(&element)`, and `focus_onward()`, the focus on to the next stop of Tab after where it is or was, or to the one before where nothing follows. `ui::LinkPreview::return_focus(&element)`, and the focus going on with none. See [moving the focus from a handler](docs/src/content/docs/ctx.md#moving-the-focus-from-a-handler).
- `app::open_url(url)`, `Ctx::open_url`, `App::open_url`: an address opened in the app the system keeps for it (`ShellExecuteW`, `NSWorkspace`, `UIApplication`, `xdg-open`, `window.open`), only one with a scheme and never a `file:` one; `open_url::stand_in` for tests and tools. `ui::Link::href(url)` opens its address after `on_click` is told, unless a handler refuses. See [opening an address](docs/src/content/docs/ctx.md#opening-an-address).
- `components::Text::rich(spans)`, a paragraph of runs of their own style, and `Text::object(element)`: an element that stands in a line of rich text at an object character (`RichTextSpan::object`), as wide as it is, wrapping with the words, on the middle of a line that keeps its height, and reached by the pointer, the focus and tooling; a selection and a highlight go across it as across a word, and a copy gives what the element says it copies (`.copy_text(..)` on any element), never the object character. See [elements in a line](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#elements-in-a-line).
- A click is the action, a tap is the gesture: `on_click` from every input, `on_tap` a finger's or a pen's, told first and each once. `TapEvent::count` (1, 2, 3 for taps in a row, on the clock of presses) and `on_double_tap`; a click waits for a second tap only on an element with `on_double_tap`. `MouseEvent::pointer_kind` says `Mouse`, `Touch` or `Pen`, and a pen taps and clicks as a finger. See [A Click Is The Action, A Tap Is The Gesture](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#a-click-is-the-action-a-tap-is-the-gesture).
- `app::Pointer` (`Fine`, `Coarse`): what the person points with, a fact of the device beside the theme. It starts as the platform says (`Pointer::platform_default()`: `Coarse` on iOS and Android, `Fine` on a desktop, in the browser the `(pointer: coarse)` media query) and follows the last input: a finger makes it coarse, a mouse or a trackpad that moves fine, a pen leaves it. `App::set_pointer` pins it, `App::follow_pointer(bool)` turns the following on and off, `App::pointer()` and `ui::current_pointer()` read it in a render, which is run again when it changes, and `Tree::set_pointer` / `Tree::pointer` are for a test. The MCP tool `lurq_pointer` reads and sets it. See [A Finger Or A Mouse](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#a-finger-or-a-mouse).
- The touch form: whether a component is dressed for a finger (the lightbox's phone viewer, the picture's phone form, the video player's touch form) is the person's pointer and nothing else, said by `ui::touch_form()`, true under `Pointer::Coarse`; neither the window's width nor the theme's density chooses it. A narrow window of a desktop keeps the pointer's form, laid out in its room: a `ui::Lightbox` is no longer the phone's viewer over a window narrower than the theme's `Sm`, and an open one keeps the form it opened in until it closes. `ui::VideoPlayer::touch(bool)` still chooses, and with none it follows the pointer. The gallery's playgrounds have a hand that points with a finger. See [The Touch Form](docs/src/content/docs/design-system.md#the-touch-form).
- `app::events::EditCommand` (`Undo`, `Redo`, `Cut`, `Copy`, `Paste`, `SelectAll`) and `KeyboardEvent::edit_command()`: the one place lurq knows the keys every app shares, Ctrl (Cmd on macOS and iOS) with Z, Shift+Z, Y (not on a Mac), X, C, V and A, and Ctrl+Insert, Shift+Insert and Shift+Delete, never with Alt, read by the letter where the key says a Latin one and by the key's place where it says another, so Ctrl+Z works in a Ukrainian layout and on a German keyboard. `EditCommand::accelerator()` is the combination to show. lurq runs a command on what has the focus: a focused text input's own editing first, then the focused element's handlers and its ancestors', innermost first, until one takes it. See [Editing commands](docs/src/content/docs/styling-events.md#editing-commands-undo-redo-copy-cut-paste).
- `.on_undo`, `.on_redo` and `.on_select_all` (and `off_*`) on every element, told an `EditEvent` that a handler with nothing to do passes on (`event.pass_on()`) to the elements around: a pad with nothing to take back inside a form whose app keeps its own history.
- `Ctx::edit(EditCommand)`, `Ctx::edit_runner()` with `EditRunner::run` for a handler, and `WindowHandle::edit(EditCommand)`: an Edit menu's or a toolbar's Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All, run on what has the focus as the key would, once the event that asked is handled.
- **Every word lurq writes by itself is in the app's locale.** `core::Locale` has six parts more beside `dates` and `media`, each in English, Ukrainian, German, French, Spanish and Polish: `common` (`CommonWords`: "Close", "Clear", "More", "Try again", "No results", a count chosen), `fields` (`FieldWords`), `navigation` (`NavigationWords`), `data` (`DataWords`: tables, filters, sums, charts), `editing` (`EditingWords`) and `keys` (`KeyWords`: "Strg" on a German keyboard, "Maj" on a French one). Every component, the bottom tab bar, the navigation bar, Markdown's notes, `files::format_size` and the items AppKit puts in a Mac's menu of the app read them from `App::set_locale`, and are drawn again when it changes; a component's single-word methods (`close_label`, `empty_text`, `count_text`) still win. A count is a `core::Plural` (one, few, many, other) said in its language's form by `Locale::count` and `PluralRule` ("22 рядки", "25 рядків"), and `core::fill` puts numbers into a word's places (`{at}`, `{total}`).
- The words of `ui::SignaturePad`, `ui::AngleSlider`, `ui::QrCode`, `ui::Transfer` and `ui::Cascader` are in the app's locale, in its six languages: `core::InputWords` (`Locale::input`: what a control that takes a value is called, its buttons' names, the words under a line, what an empty list says, "Loading") and `core::DisplayWords` (`Locale::display`: what tooling calls a QR code), with `Locale::with_input` and `with_display`. Each takes them from `ui::current_locale()` and has `.locale(&Locale)`; a word told by itself (`placeholder`, `add_label`, `clear_label` and the others) stays above both.
- `ui::Watermark`: a text, or a picture (`media`), repeated across what it covers, turned and faint, over the content: `gap`, `angle`, `offset`, `stagger` and the mark's part as the board draws them. No pointer hits the marks and tooling does not read them; they are one mark in the tree, painted by the engine only where they can be seen, so a watermark over a long scrolled page costs the marks in view
- `.repeat(Repeat)` on any element paints what it holds again and again across its box, a gap apart, every other row moved by a stagger, only where it can be seen: a pattern, a watermark. The copies are paint alone
- An element described `a11y_hidden` `true` is left out of `lurq_read_tree` (but with `filter: "all"`) and `lurq_inspect` with all it holds, and its text is no part of a control's name: a watermark's marks, a chart's axis
- `ui::QrCode`: a text an app gives drawn as a code a phone reads, dark on light in either appearance (its roles read in the light palette), with `level(QrLevel)`, `length`, `quiet_zone`, a logo (`media`) and `label`; to tooling an image described by its text. Its modules are drawn on whole device pixels. The encoder is lurq's own (byte mode, versions 1 to 40, four levels), and `ui::QrMatrix::encode` gives the modules themselves
- `.snap_to_pixels(true)` on any element draws its fills and borders, and those of everything in it, on whole device pixels: crisp at 150%, and two boxes that meet still meet
- `Theme::palette_color_for(appearance, role)` reads a role in the palette of one appearance, whatever the theme is in
- `TextInput::squiggles(InputSquiggles)` draws wavy lines under ranges of an input's text, what a spelling checker marks, on every row a range is on; `TextInput::ghost(InputGhost)` draws a suggestion after the end of what is typed, while the input has the focus with the caret there.
- `ui::Input::squiggles(ranges)` and `ui::Textarea::squiggles(ranges)`, in `Destructive`, and `ui::Input::completion(&str)`, a suggestion after what is typed that Tab takes: the marks of text editing the board draws (Desktop Tools, Text Editing), on the design system's fields. The board's edit menu is `ContextMenu::bar(true)`.
- `ui::RichTextEditor` (with the `markdown` feature): formatted text typed into a box under a bar of its tools. Its value is a `RichText`, bound (`new(Signal<RichText>)`) or held by the app (`controlled`), with `on_change`. The tools (`RichTextTool`: the style of the paragraph, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, a bulleted and a numbered list, a quote, code, a link, an image, undo and redo) work on the selection and show what it is; the keys do the same (Ctrl+B, I, U, Shift+X, E, K, Alt+0 to 3, Shift+7, 8, 9); Markdown typed becomes what it says (`## `, `- `, `1. `, `> `, `**bold**`, `*italic*`, `` `code` ``, `~~struck~~`). `RichTextController` runs its commands from outside it. It has a placeholder, rows, read only, disabled, the judgments, a footer that counts the words, and every word it draws can be said otherwise.
- `ui::Editable`: a text read where it stands and edited in place. A press, Enter or F2 puts a field where the text was, with the text selected; Enter and leaving the field keep the edit, Escape drops it, and the focus goes back to the reading form. Bound (`new(Signal<String>)`) or held (`controlled`), with `on_change`, `on_cancel`, `editing(Signal<bool>)` and `on_editing_change`, a placeholder, its two buttons or none (`actions`), `submit_on_blur`, read only and disabled.
- `TextInput::paragraphs(kinds, styles)` (with the `markdown` feature): a styled input keeps what each of its paragraphs is in a `Signal<Vec<ParagraphKind>>` beside its spans (`Body`, `Heading(1..=6)`, `Bullet`, `Ordered`, `Quote`, `Code`), in step with every edit and in its undo history, and draws each kind as a `ParagraphStyles` says: its type, the room before its text, a `ParagraphMarker` (a dot, a number, a bar), a fill, and the room between paragraphs. `TextInputController::set_paragraphs`, `selected_paragraphs`, `caret_paragraph` and `rich_text`; `StyledChange::paragraphs()` and `to_rich_text()`.
- `components::RichText`: the text, the spans, the kinds and the objects of a styled input as one value, with `from_markdown`, `to_markdown`, `plain`, `with_objects`, `blocks`, `plain_text` and `word_count`.
- A styled input holds things that are not text (with the `markdown` feature): `TextInput::objects(signal, element)` binds a `Signal<Vec<InlineObject>>`, a record for each `OBJECT_CHARACTER` (U+FFFC) of the value with its byte, its source, its label and its width, and builds the element drawn for one. The element is a child of the input, measured in the room the text has and placed where its character stands: its size is its box in the row, which is as high as its tallest box and keeps the text's line at its foot; a box that does not fit goes to the next row, and one wider than its row is as wide as the row. To the caret an object is one character: the arrows go past it, a press selects it, Backspace after it selects it and the next deletes it, and a selected one is under a veil of the selection's color. It scrolls and is cut with the text, and the pointer and the focus do not reach what it holds. The input moves the records with every edit, keeps them in its undo history and reports them (`StyledChange::objects()`); `TextInputController::insert_object` and `objects` work them from outside. `RichText::from_markdown` reads an image (`![label](source)`, `<img src alt width>`) as an object, where it read it as its alternative text linked to its source, and `to_markdown` writes it back; `rich_clipboard` carries it as that Markdown. See [Objects](docs/src/content/docs/text-input-styled.md#objects).
- `TextInput::resizable_objects(bool)` (with the `markdown` feature): an object of a styled input selected alone has a handle on its lower corner, and a drag of it sets the object's `width` as it goes, as one undo step reported once. `ui::RichTextEditor` has it on its pictures. Enter with a picture selected in the editor starts a paragraph after it, and a row keeps the room of a caret after a picture as wide as the row.
- `ui::RichTextEditor::on_paste_image(keep)`: a picture pasted as pixels, a screenshot, is given to the app to keep, and the picture of the source it answers with is put at the caret; files that are pictures, pasted from a file manager, are put in by their paths.
- The MCP server's `lurq_interact` `paste` takes an `image`, the path of an image file whose pixels the paste holds, as a screenshot on the clipboard would be: an agent tests what an app does with a pasted picture without the person's clipboard. See [Pastes and file drags](docs/src/content/docs/mcp.md#pastes-and-file-drags).
- `ui::RichTextEditor` holds pictures in its document: `RichTextController::insert_picture(source, label)` (and `insert_object`, for one with a width) puts one at the caret, which is what an app's `on_image` does with the file a person chose; a picture is drawn by a `ui::Picture` of its source, or by what `RichTextEditor::picture(..)` builds. It is selected, deleted, undone, copied and pasted with the words around it, alone in its paragraph or among words.
- `TextInput::rich_clipboard(bool)` (with the `markdown` feature) keeps the formatting of a styled input with kinds of paragraphs through the clipboard, and `ui::RichTextEditor` has it: a copy or a cut writes the selection's Markdown beside its text, and the system's clipboard is given the Markdown where the selection has a mark or a kind, so another app or another lurq process reads it as written; an input of the same app without the option still pastes the plain text. A paste with Markdown beside its text, or whose text reads as Markdown (`RichText::reads_as_markdown`: a block mark at the start of a line, or a mark Markdown reads), comes with its marks and kinds as one undo step; a paste into a line of code is plain. `ClipboardContent::with_markdown` and `markdown_content`, `PasteEvent::markdown`, `ClipboardWriter::write_rich`, `ClipboardReader::read_markdown` and `TextInputController::replace_selection_rich` carry it. See [Copy, paste and links](docs/src/content/docs/text-input-styled.md#copy-paste-and-links).
- A row of a paragraph with a kind in a styled input is as high as its kind's line height says, whatever the font's own line is; an empty input with kinds draws its placeholder in the kind of its first paragraph, after its indent and its mark; and kinds are drawn where the input wraps its lines and is not justified, every paragraph as body text elsewhere, the kinds still kept.
- `ui::Select::fit_options(bool)` and `SelectStyle::menu_fits_options(bool)`: the list of a select as wide as its longest option, with the room of the check, in place of as wide as its trigger. The rich text editor's style control is as wide as the name it shows, and its list fits its styles.
- `ThemeCaret` says how wide and how high a text input's caret is and how thick the line under a composition: `width`, `height` (`CaretHeight::FontSize(share)` or `CaretHeight::TextBand`) and `composition_line`, with `with_*` and `set_*` for each, `with_mode` and `ThemeCaret::legacy()`.
- An underline mark in a styled input: `SpanStyle::underline()`, `TextMark::Underline`, `ThemeTextMarks::underline`.
- `TextInputController::undo`, `redo`, `can_undo` and `can_redo`: what an undo button runs and is enabled by, on a plain input too.
- `TextInput::select_on_focus(bool)`: all its text is selected as it takes the focus, so that what is typed takes its place.
- `ui::Button::focuses(&ElementRef)` and `ui::Toggle::focuses(&ElementRef)`: a press of it gives the focus to another element in place of taking it, as the tools of an editor's bar leave it in the text.
- `ui::Notification`: something that happened which stays where the app lists it. A card (the board's: a bell or who it is from, a title and a line, a time, buttons, what dismisses it) and a row of a list (`NotificationVariant::Row`: the unread dot, an avatar, the text, the time), `unread`, `on_click` for a press of the whole and `on_close` for what dismisses it.
- `ui::NotificationCenter`: the panel that lists an app's notifications beside its bell (`trigger`, a `Badge` with its count on a `Button`), with "Mark all read", "Clear all" and "View all" telling `on_mark_all_read`, `on_clear` and `on_view_all`, a list that scrolls, an empty panel, and the arrow keys between its rows. The list and what was read stay the app's.
- `ui::TrayPopover` with `TrayAction`, `TrayToggle` and `TrayStatusVariant`: the panel a tray icon's press opens, with the app's mark, its title and status, rows with a switch and rows that run a command. It is the panel in the flow, for a window of its own, or `anchored(open, anchor)` over the app's window. lurq has no tray icon API.
- `ui::Tour` has `Tour::controlled(step, on_change)` beside the bound form, `on_change(Option<usize>)`, a `skip_button` with `skip_label`, and takes the focus (`takes_focus`): into its card as it starts, on the pressed button from step to step, back to what had it as it ends. The Right and Left arrows, Home and End step it while the focus is in its card. A step's buttons have the board's words of 13 and its card the medium shadow. `ui::TeachingTip` has `tertiary(label, handler)`, a quiet button before the others.
- `Overlay::traps_tab(bool)` and `Popup::traps_tab(bool)`: an overlay that holds Tab while the focus is in it, as a modal does, and closes nothing behind it. From its last element Tab goes to its first and Shift+Tab the other way; Escape, a press outside and the return of the focus are as they were. `ui::Tour` turns it on for its card (`Tour::traps_tab(false)` turns it off), and `ui::TeachingTip` has it off (`TeachingTip::traps_tab`).
- `Tree::set_press_clock(Option<Instant>)`, for tests: the time presses are given at, by which a double press, a double or triple click in text and a click held back for a double click are measured. A table and a data grid tell the second press of a double press by the time the window was given each press, where they read the clock as each was handled: a first press that took long to handle parted the two. So do a tree view and a column view (a double press activates), a video player's picture (a double press asks for fullscreen) and `WindowChrome`'s title bar (a double press maximizes), and a click held back for a double click is held from the time its press was given.
- A popup or an overlay that takes the focus and is given another anchor while it is open is the same popup: it does not take the focus again, and gives it back, as it closes, to what had it before it first opened. It was taken for one that closed and another that opened, so the focus went to its first element at each change of anchor and back to nothing in the end.
- `ui::PathField`: the path of a folder or a file in an input with a button beside it that browses for one (the board's Path Field). The button tells `on_browse`; a field told what its path is of (`picks(PathKind::Folder | File | SaveFile)`) and mounted opens the system's dialog itself, starting where the path is, sets what is chosen on its signal and tells `on_pick`, and takes a folder or a file dropped on it the same way. It has an input's sizes (the small one unless told), judgments, read only and disabled, is a control a `Field` holds, and its words can be said otherwise (`browse_label`).
